#2266. Aarhus vs Braga - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joshua Ojele | 04 Nov 2026 02:15, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 13:37

Danish outfit Aarhus will look to make home advantage count when they go head-to-head with Portuguese side Braga at Cepheus Park in matchday three of the Conference League on Thursday.

With just one point from the opening two games, the home side will be eager to finally get off the mark, while the Portuguese outfit will look to build on a dominant league display at the weekend and continue from where they left off against Gent a fortnight ago.

What seemed like a resurgent domestic spell was met with disappointment at the weekend as Aarhus suffered a 2-0 defeat at the hands of Brondby when the two sides met at Brondby Stadium on Sunday.

Having failed to taste victory in the opening nine league games, De Hvii'e bounced back with two victories and one draw from their subsequent three outings, seeing off SonderjyskE and Viborg in that run, before strikes from Marko Divkovic and Mads Frokjaer saw them crash back to earth on Sunday.

Aarhus' struggles have extended beyond the league, with the Danish outfit managing just one point from their first two Conference League games and in need of a lift ahead of more challenging opponents on their schedule.

Jakob Poulsen’s men kicked off their Conference League journey with a 1-0 defeat against Belgian outfit Gent on October 15, one week before playing out a 1-1 stalemate with Egnatia, with James Bogere scoring in the 49th minute to cancel out Ildi Gruda’s 13th-minute opener and force a share of the spoils.

After back-to-back games on the road, home comfort now awaits Aarhus, who will fancy their chances in front of their supporters, having picked up two wins and two draws from four matches at Cepheus Park since September 5.

Over in Portugal, Braga responded to their disappointing cup exit with a commanding team display at the weekend when they secured a 3-0 victory over Famalicao in the second of their cup-and-league double header.

Following a goalless first half at the Estadio Municipal de Braga, the home side upped the ante after the break, with Ricardo Horta and Pau Vitor netting on either side of Vitor Rofino’s 67th-minute own goal to sink the Vila Nova and rise into eighth place in the Primeira Liga standings.

This was a timely boost for Braga ahead of their return to action in Europe, having lost five of their previous six matches across all competitions, including successive Primeira Liga defeats against Sporting CP, Gil Vicente and Nacional, and a penalty-shootout loss to Famalicao in the Taca da Liga quarter-finals on October 29.

Attention now turns to the Conference League, where Franclim Carvalho’s men were denied a dream start to the campaign on October 15, when they suffered a 3-0 hammering at the hands of Swiss outfit Copenhagen, with Mohamed Elyounoussi opening the scoring in the 49th minute before Andreas Cornelius netted a late brace to cap off an impressive opening-day display.

However, Os Arcebispos bounced back one week later against Gent, claiming a 2-0 victory over the Belgian outfit at the Estadio Municipal de Braga thanks to goals from Vitor Gomez and Gabriel Martine on either side of the half-time break.

Questions remain over Braga’s ability to deliver on the road, and they will need to be at their best at Cepheus Park, having failed to taste victory in their most recent four away matches while managing just one win in eight since the start of August.

Aarhus will take to the pitch without Swedish defender Eric Kahl, who is currently suspended after picking up a red card against Egnatia in matchday two on October 22.

While Kahl’s absence represents a major setback at the defensive end of the pitch, Poulsen should rely on Iceland international Daniel Gretarsson, with the 31-year-old coming into the fold to form the back three with Colin Rosler and Frederik Tingager.

On the injury front, Aarhus remain without the midfield duo of Nicolai Poulsen and Kevin Yakob, who have been ruled out through knee and Achilles tendon injuries respectively.

As for Braga, Sikou Niakate continues his long road to full fitness after sustaining an Achilles tendon injury back in April and the 27-year-old defender will play no part in Thursday’s tie.

Off the back of a roaring team display against Famalicao, Carvalho could name a similar XI in Denmark, with Mario Dorgeles, Horta, Gabriel Martinez and Fran Navarro forming the attacking quartet, while Vitor Gomez and Adrian Leon Barisic should retain their roles at the defensive end of the pitch.

Hansen; Rosler, Tingager, Gretarsson; Carstensen, Arnstad, Solbakken, Andersen; Jorgensen, McCowatt; Bogere

Fontes; Gomez, Barisic, Lagerbielke, Arrey-Mbi; Gorby, Moutinho; Dorgeles, Horta, Martinez; Navarro

Having stumbled into the campaign, Aarhus will be eager to finally kickstart their European journey in front of their home supporters, but Braga’s experience in Europe could prove valuable in what is expected to be a well-contested encounter. The Portuguese outfit boast superior quality across this pitch, and we expect that this should see them emerge with the result here, albeit by the odd goal.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Certificata la ripartenza dopo la mancata qualificazione al Mondiale, la Nazionale tornerà a radunarsi domenica sera a Coverciano in vista delle ultime due gare del girone di Nations League. Il doppio successo ottenuto nella lunga finestra di settembre/ottobre con la Turchia e il pareggio in rimonta nel match di Saint-Denis con la Francia hanno permesso agli Azzurri di portarsi al secondo posto in classifica nel Gruppo 1 della Lega A a quota sette punti, tre in meno della capolista Francia e uno in più del Belgio: e proprio i Bleus e i Diavoli Rossi saranno gli avversari dell’Italia nelle sfide in programma giovedì 12 novembre allo stadio Giuseppe Meazza di Milano (emessi 57.000 biglietti, in vendita gli ultimi 3.000 tagliandi) e domenica 15 novembre allo stadio Re Baldovino di Bruxelles.

Già sicura almeno del terzo posto e di essere quindi tra le teste di serie al sorteggio per le qualificazioni europee in programma il prossimo 6 dicembre a Belfast, l’Italia va a caccia di altri punti per ipotecare la permanenza nella Lega A (le due migliori terze classificate si salvano senza bisogno di ricorrere agli spareggi) e con l’ambizione di chiudere il girone nelle prime due posizioni conquistando così il pass per i quarti di finale.

Se per le prime quattro gare – racchiuse nell’arco di undici giorni – Roberto Mancini aveva puntato su un gruppo allargato di 34 calciatori, stavolta il Ct ne ha convocati 31. Quattro giovani sono alla prima chiamata in Nazionale maggiore: il centrocampista classe 2009 del Como Riccardo Cassano, il centrocampista classe 2008 del Parma Simone Lontani, l’attaccante classe 2006 del Milan Alphadjo Cisse e l’attaccante classe 2007 del Como Mattia Liberali.

Per quanto riguarda Cassano e Lontani, Mancini ha voluto convocarli per poterli osservare da vicino e per dar loro una preziosa opportunità per mettersi in mostra: non resteranno aggregati al gruppo per tutta la durata del raduno, ma nei prossimi giorni raggiungeranno le Nazionali Under 18 e Under 19, impegnate rispettivamente nel Mondiale al via il 19 novembre in Qatar e nella seconda fase delle qualificazioni al Campionato Europeo in programma dall’11 al 17 novembre nelle Marche.

#2262. Argentina, scelta pesante rimandata: a novembre la 10 di Messi rimarrà vacante

L'Argentina si appresta a inaugurare uno dei capitoli più significativi e delicati della sua storia recente. Dopo quasi due decenni trascorsi nel segno di Lionel Messi, sua massima figura e condottiero nella conquista della Coppa del Mondo FIFA 2022, l'Albiceleste disputerà i suoi primi impegni internazionali senza il fuoriclasse di Rosario. A catalizzare l'attenzione della vigilia è stata tuttavia una scelta simbolica ben precisa: la maglia numero 10 rimarrà infatti vacante e non verrà assegnata a nessun calciatore nel corso delle imminenti amichevoli previste dal calendario. Lo riferisce BeIN Sports.

La squadra guidata dal commissario tecnico Lionel Scaloni scenderà in campo il 13 novembre alla Bombonera contro l'Uruguay per poi sfidare la Costa Rica il 17 novembre a Cordoba, sfruttando la maggiore flessibilità che il regolamento concede sulla numerazione dei dorsali nei match non ufficiali. Lo scenario è destinato però a mutare in vista delle Qualificazioni Sudamericane del 2027: a quel punto l'Argentina si troverà costretta dai regolamenti a individuare un nuovo erede per la sua casacca più emblematica.

La decisione di lasciare temporaneamente intatta la numero 10 evoca un celebre antecedente storico risalente al 2001, quando l'Associazione del Football Argentino (AFA), allora presieduta da Julio Grondona, tentò di ritirare la maglia per tributare un omaggio eterno a Diego Armando Maradona, eroe dei Mondiali di Messico 1986. L'iniziativa fu tuttavia fermata dalla FIFA, che prima della spedizione di Corea-Giappone 2002 pretese una numerazione rigorosamente consecutiva dal 1 al 23. A distanza di oltre vent'anni, la storia vuole che qualcun altro debba forzatamente indossare la diez dell'Albiceleste.

#2261. Verdict split in Watford vs Middlesbrough predictions as hosts show much-needed ‘grit’

Watford have endured a tough start to the Championship season under head coach Alessio Dionisi. They are positioned in 20th place in the table after 14 fixtures and are four points above the bottom three.

The Hornets won 1-0 at home against Lincoln City last time out which was a much needed victory. Summer signing Stephy Mavididi got their winner against the Imps in Hertfordshire.

Middlesbrough lost 2-0 away at Wrexham on Wednesday night and were without key striker Will Lankshear in North Wales due to injury. Boro missed his firepower in front of goal and slumped to a disappointing defeat at the Racecourse Ground.

Nevertheless, Boro remain top of the division as they eye promotion to the Premier League under manager Kim Hellberg in this campaign. They are one point above Wolves in 2nd and two ahead of Birmingham City, Stoke City and Swansea City.

“Watford’s win over Lincoln City last time out and eased some pressure off head coach Alessio Dionisi. They have now won their last two home games which should give them confidence ahead of this weekend.

“Despite their struggles, the Hornets do have some good individual players at their disposal like Stephy Mavididi, Iker Bravo and Othmane Maamma. They can cause problems for any opponents on their day when they are at it.

“Middlesbrough will be desperate to bounce back from their loss at Wrexham. They are a different team without Will Lankshear and if he is unavailable again on Saturday, they could be in trouble.

“Nevertheless, Boro are top for a reason and their defeat in Wales was their first one in the league since August in a run of spanning back 11 games. I think they may have to settle for a point in Hertfordshire though.”

“Watford have found a couple of important results in their last two home games. They haven’t been pretty but they’ve shown grit and got points on the board, which will matter in a battle against relegation to the Championship.

“This is a big task though, and Middlesbrough will be out for vengeance. Boro were really poor at Wrexham in the week, as they were far less efficient with their 62.2% possession than we have seen this campaign to date.

“When Kim Hellberg’s side last lost, they responded with a comprehensive 3-1 win over West Brom and went on an 11-game unbeaten run. They’ll be eager to bounce back in similar fashion, and I think they’ll be able to despite the Hornets’ improvements at home.

“I’m going for a comfortable away win as the visitors produce a strong response to their Wrexham disappointment.”

#2259. Atalanta BC vs. Kairat - prediction, team news, lineups

By Seye Omidiora | 04 Nov 2026 04:48, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 05:02

Atalanta BC need to respond to Monday's humiliating 3-0 Serie A defeat to Parma when they host Kairat in the Conference League on Thursday.

The defeat prompted Maurizio Sarri to criticise his players' mentality as they entered a "ritiro" ahead of facing a Kairat side now managed by Zhora Arutyunyan following Rafael Urazbakhtin's sudden resignation.

Atalanta had gone four matches without winning before Parma visited Bergamo on Monday night, but the ensuing defeat was still jarring.

The Emilians were winless in four matches, losing three and failing to score in three while languishing in the bottom three; however, Atalanta's collapse in front of their own supporters angered Sarri enough to make a training retreat his response.

Players are no longer permitted to return home ahead of Thursday's European visit from Kairat, as La Dea seek to arrest a worrying slump that has brought three defeats in five matches and six goals conceded across the other two fixtures, draws of 4-4 and 2-2 with Frosinone and Fiorentina respectively.

Sarri's men have conceded 14 goals across their five-match winless run, letting in at least two in every fixture to underline their frailties ahead of Conference League matchday three.

The Bergamaschi sit 15th after two rounds, winning once before losing 2-0 to Ajax last time out, but their position in Europe's third-tier club competition is far from irretrievable.

Kairat, though, are in a worse position than the Nerazzurri, with the Kazakhstani outfit winless after two matches following a draw with Riga on matchday one and a heavy 5-1 home defeat to Panathinaikos in the previous round.

That Conference League defeat on October 22 formed part of a three-match winless run and a sequence of one victory in four fixtures, leading to Urazbakhtin's resignation and Arutyunyan's subsequent appointment.

The two-time title-winning manager's departure was unexpected, but the Yellow Blacks cannot dwell on it for long, with their continental prospects at risk of further damage if they suffer consecutive defeats in the competition this week.

Despite Atalanta's own struggles, Kairat's prospects appear bleak given their failure to win any of their last four European away fixtures across Champions League and Europa League qualifying and Conference League action.

Sary-Qaralar suffered 1-0 defeats to both Omonia and Levski Sofia and lost 3-0 to Anderlecht before ending their scoreless run and sequence of defeats with a 1-1 draw at Riga last month.

Those struggles may therefore give Atalanta hope of a response, although the Yellow Blacks will seek an improved performance to compound the Bergamaschi's misery during their extended winless run.

Franck Kessie (calf), Sead Kolasinac (thigh), Giacomo Raspadori (thigh) and Gianluca Scamacca (thigh) are believed to be touch-and-go for Atalanta, while Thomas Kristensen (muscle), Raoul Bellanova (thigh), Isak Hien (muscle), Odilon Kossounou (thigh) and Gianluca Gaetano (muscle) are sidelined as the hosts contend with a spate of muscular injuries.

Nikola Krstovic remains Atalanta's leading goal threat with six Serie A strikes, and the forward could make his first European appearance of the season if Scamacca is not ready to start.

Kairat have fewer injury concerns, with Jorginho the visitors' only absentee, having not featured since July.

While Kairat have had two different goalscorers in the Conference League, Marc Gual and Edmilson have both reached double figures domestically, combining for 22 league goals to establish themselves as Sary-Qaralar's leading attacking threats.

Carnesecchi; Zappacosta, Scalvini, Obric, Bernasconi; Ederson, Pasalic; Elmas, De Ketelaere, Rowe; Krstovic

Anarbekov; Tapalov, Martynovich, Shirobokov, Mata; Mendonsa, Oksanen; Mrynskiy, Gual, Tuyakbaev; Edmilson

Atalanta's humiliation against Parma makes a convincing response far from certain, but Kairat's return of one goal from their last four European away matches offers the hosts some encouragement.

With Krstovic potentially leading the attack, La Dea should have enough quality to end their five-match winless run and ease the pressure on Sarri.

#2258. La Russa contro lo sciopero del tifo: "Lazio prima, come fanno a non andare allo stadio?"

La Lazio è prima in classifica, ma una parte della tifoseria continua a protestare contro il presidente Claudio Lotito attraverso lo sciopero del tifo. Una scelta che Ignazio La Russa, presidente del Senato, fatica a comprendere. Intervenuto nel corso di AdnTalks, in un'intervista rilasciata all'Adnkronos, La Russa ha invitato i sostenitori biancocelesti a non rinunciare al sostegno alla squadra, pur riconoscendo il loro diritto di contestare la proprietà.

#2257. Dave Challinor confirms Championship job desire and rival interest before Sheffield Wednesday swoop

Sheffield Wednesday officially announced Dave Challinor as their new boss earlier this week. The move marked the 51-year-old’s first job since leaving Stockport County in the summer, but his success with the Hatters meant SWFC weren’t alone in eyeing him.

Speaking to The Star, Challinor revealed other clubs wanted ‘conversations’, and his eyes were initially on a step up to the Championship before joining the Owls – a club with ambitions of returning to the second tier. He said: “Without going into too much detail, obviously people wanted to have conversations. I was in the fortunate position where I didn’t need to jump back in. I came out of a really good football club, and we had success, so I had the luxury of being able to be a little bit selective and wait.

“My natural progression would have been to try and get a Championship opportunity. That made the number of clubs I am appealing to was probably pretty small. The next opportunity either needed to give me that chance, or be one I could grow with. I certainly couldn’t have picked a bigger one in terms of this club and the amount of growth it’s got in it.”

Challinor was more than justified in initially eyeing a step up to the Championship after his Stockport County departure. He took the Hatters from the National League to the brink of promotion from League One, but there was a mutual agreement that it was a good time to part ways.

His back-to-back top six finishes in League One and promotion pedigree made him the ideal candidate for Sheffield Wednesday. And, if the Chester-born boss wasn’t going to land a Championship job, moving to a third-tier side with promotion ambitions made for the next best option.

Arguably though, the SWFC job is more exciting than many in the Championship. The ongoing rebuild under new ownership and desire to wake this sleeping giant makes the managerial post a highly attractive one, so Challinor is progressing in job stature despite staying in the same league.

Challinor is a coup for the Owls, and it’s an exciting step for him too. It’s a win-win appointment that benefits all parties, as long as results follow.

Challinor watched on from the stands as Sheffield Wednesday played out a 1-1 draw with Blackpool under Matt Hamshaw last week. The new boss has been able to get to work with his squad this week though, and the first game is just around the corner.

SWFC face League Two side Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup on Sunday. It marks a favourable start for Challinor, both in terms of the level of the opposition and the fact that there aren’t points at stake.

David Storch has been eager to stress that this appointment is part of a long-term rebuild, and success will not be defined by how this season goes. Nevertheless, Challinor will be determined to get Wednesday firing as soon as he can to strengthen their case for a spot in League One’s top six at the very least.

#2256. Chelsea: ‘Great to watch’ - Gerrard hails Estevao and predicts Xabi Alonso headache

By Axel Clody | 04 Nov 2026 06:51

Steven Gerrard did not hold back in his praise of Estevao after Chelsea’s 2-0 win over Manchester United at Stamford Bridge on Saturday.

Impressed by the Brazilian’s display, the former Liverpool captain highlighted the forward’s attacking personality and pointed to a problem Xabi Alonso will have to solve in the coming months: the competition for places in Chelsea’s attack, especially when Joao Pedro is available again.

Estevao was one of the standout performers in the London side’s Premier League victory. Handed his first Premier League start of the season, the 19-year-old stayed on the pitch until the 87th minute and left to applause from the home fans. Involved throughout, particularly in the first half, the Brazilian had six shots, three of them on target, and created three chances for team-mates.

The performance could have been even more memorable had the two goals he scored not been ruled out for offside. Even without officially hitting the net, the forward showed exactly the qualities Gerrard considers important in a front player: initiative, directness and a willingness to finish moves.

Appearing on The Overlap Daily, Gerrard showed enthusiasm for the football played by the Brazilian. The former player valued both the technical quality and the way Estevao looks to settle moves, without needlessly prolonging attacking build-up.

‘He’s really exciting. He’s great to watch. Your typical Brazilian where it’s about flair, it’s about quality, but I like that he is very direct. I like that he’s hungry to get shots off.’

The forward’s directness was one of the aspects the Englishman stressed most. Comparing Estevao’s approach with what he sees as a problem for some Liverpool players in attack, Gerrard highlighted the importance of turning chances into shots, without insisting excessively on the search for the perfect move.

‘If there is any criticism to be made of some of the Liverpool players in attack, I would say they don’t shoot enough. They are looking for that extra pass or that touch of perfection, whereas what I like about Estevao is when he comes in from the left, plays a little combination and crosses the ball.’

Gerrard also mentioned an important save by Senne Lammens, the Manchester United goalkeeper, from one of the Brazilian’s shots. For him, even in situations where he could opt for a more elaborate move, Estevao shows a constant desire to attack the opposition goal.

‘It’s a fantastic save from the United keeper, but I like the way he is hungry to get shots off. There was a chance in the first half where he could have slowed the game down and tried to pull the ball back to the edge of the box, but he wanted to have that little bit of urge to go and score a goal.’

The praise, however, came with a prediction about the competition the Brazilian will face at Chelsea. With Joao Pedro recovering from a thigh injury, Gerrard believes Alonso will have a tricky task organising the attacking options available.

‘So, he’s desperate to impress the manager, and he’s going to give Xabi a problem when Joao Pedro comes back, because Pedro Neto’s a great player as well, but five doesn’t go into that front four, so it’s going to be interesting’ - he concluded.

Gerrard’s recognition comes at an important moment for Estevao.

Since Xabi Alonso took charge of Chelsea, the Brazilian has struggled to string together a run of games and accumulate minutes. His limited involvement fuelled questions about the space reserved for the forward, who arrived at the club amid high expectation after standing out in Brazilian football.

Questions about the youngster’s situation have become frequent in the Spanish manager’s interviews, especially given the gap between the potential the player has shown and the opportunities he has actually been given. Estevao himself, while away with Brazil, was asked about his limited minutes but avoided any public confrontation and said he was focused on earning his place.

The numbers help to gauge the change brought by the Manchester United game. Before the match, Estevao had played six Premier League games, totalling just 47 minutes. In the EFL Cup, he had started twice, with 125 minutes on the pitch. In midweek, however, he did not even leave the bench in Chelsea’s elimination by Liverpool. At Stamford Bridge, he was given 87 minutes to show his football.

Alonso had previously signalled that the intensity and physical demands of the Premier League could be obstacles to the Brazilian’s adaptation. After the win over United, however, the manager made a point of praising the forward’s response, with the player having been told in advance that he would start the game.

‘We spoke after Tottenham and he knew he was going to be playing today. He had the best response and showed that he deserves more and that he was prepared. That is exactly what you expect from a player, so I am very happy for him. He deserved to enjoy the game. He had a good connection with the crowd and was really close to scoring his goal. He fought really hard and at the end he was tired, but it has been a good day for him’ - Alonso said.

L’obiettivo è individuare soluzioni concrete per uscire da una crisi certificata anche dai risultati. Queste le sue dichiarazioni: “La notizia è la scelta della volontà della proprietà di mandare la squadra in ritiro con un giorno di anticipo rispetto a quello che sarebbe stato, in ritiro per stare insieme e continuare a trovare soluzioni in un momento complicato e di crisi. Il presidente De Laurentiis è evidentemente preoccupato, da lì la scelta nella speranza che questo gruppo, parlandosi e dicendosi tutto nel faccia a faccia di domani, possa trovare le soluzioni e la forza in un momento difficile certificato dai numeri. Squadra in ritiro da domani: faccia a faccia, allenamento e tutti in ritiro”.

#2253. Exclusive: Matt Jarvis backs ‘exceptional’ Scott Parker in verdict on West Ham’s Nuno sacking

West Ham parted ways with Nuno Espirito Santo on Tuesday, and swiftly named Scott Parker as his replacement. The new boss led WHUFC for Wednesday’s 3-3 draw at Cardiff City, and many are backing him for success.

Among the supporters of Parker is former Hammers winger Matt Jarvis, who believes inconsistency ultimately cost Nuno his job. Speaking to The72 via Midnite, Jarvis said: “The fans’ perspective flipped from last year’s misery to expecting to dominate this league and win promotion. Visiting the London Stadium in front of 55,000 to 60,000 supporters is every opposition team’s cup final, and adapting to that takes time.

“With Nuno, people forget he had been there for a significant period. It took him time to establish himself in the Premier League, then they had a good spell before fading towards the end. The board backed him in the transfer market after relegation, but results remained too inconsistent. The supporters couldn’t see a clear playing identity, and the home defeat to West Brom proved the final straw.”

Jarvis went on to talk up Parker’s chances of success as West Ham boss. He picked out his communication skills, experience and passion as key traits as he looks to take the Hammers back to the Premier League.

“People will debate whether the decision came too early or whether they should have waited for the international break, but the club felt a change was needed. They have four upcoming fixtures where they should realistically target three points from every game.

“Scott Parker has a fantastic Championship track record and an existing connection to the club.

“He’s an exceptional communicator, which is vital for translating training-ground instruction onto the pitch on matchdays. He’s passionate, knows what it takes to get out of this division, and his arrival should have an immediate impact.”

Parker has won three promotions from the Championship, achieving the feat at Fulham, Bournemouth and Burnley. In his most recent triumph, he led the Clarets to 2nd place in 2024/25 and broke the league record for the fewest goals conceded in an entire campaign at just 16.

The switch from Nuno to Parker has split some opinions. There are justified concerns that their play styles are too similar given the new boss’ recent teams have been built on the foundation of a strong defence.

This was the case in his last job at Burnley, but the 46-year-old has shown he has the ability to switch up his approach. At Fulham in the 2020/21 season, his side had the second-highest average possession in the Championship with 61.4% (FotMob), as well 85 big chances created and 1,101 touches in the opposition box.

Over time, it will become clear whether Parker plans on emulating his Burnley tactics at WHUFC, or his Fulham style. Alternatively, he could switch things up completely, and if he does, ex-Hammer Jarvis believes he has the know-how and communication skills to get this across to his new squad.

After taking charge of the Cardiff City draw a matter of hours after his appointment on Wednesday, Parker leads his side for the second time against Blackburn Rovers this Saturday.

Midnite, the Principal Partner of Wolverhampton Wanderers for the 2026/27 season, are providing luxury coach travel on the “Midnite Express” for 100 Wolverhampton Wanderers supporters, as they set off on a 130-mile-round trip to watch their beloved Wolves take on the league’s glamour club, Wrexham, this Saturday, November 7.

Fans will be joined on the Express by Wolves club legends, Steve Bull, Carl Ikeme and Matt Jarvis and treated to club-centric quizzes and live Q&As, with exclusive prizes up for grabs.

#2251. Blackburn Rovers sweat over injuries to two key defenders as West Ham challenge awaits

Blackburn Rovers face West Ham in their final game before the November international break this Saturday. They come into the tie off the back of a 2-1 defeat against Stoke City, and Tony Mowbray’s side were without centre-back Tom Atcheson for the tie, while full-back Ryan Alebiosu came off in the first half.

Now, speaking to the press, Mowbray has said the Rovers duo will require further assessment to see if they can face the Hammers as they struggle with minor issues. As quoted by the club’s official media, the boss said: “Big Tom has still got a little niggle in his groin, so it’s about whether we take a chance with him or not. Ryan is going to go for a scan, and I hope it isn’t too bad.”

Alebiosu has been a mainstay for Blackburn Rovers this season, starting every Championship match and playing the full 90 minutes in all but two games. Atcheson meanwhile has made 11 appearances in all competitions, including 10 league starts.

Blackburn Rovers aren’t blessed for depth, so it would be a blow to go into Saturday’s game against West Ham without Alebiosu and Atcheson. Mowbray will have to work out a solution if either are ruled out.

Alebiosu has been the mainstay at right-back this season, but his absence would likely offer an opportunity for summer signing Gady Beyuku to impress. The 20-year-old has only made four substitute appearances to date, but he came on for 49 minutes against the Potters on Tuesday.

At centre-back, Hayden Carter partnered Sean McLoughlin with Atcheson out. The former has had injury problems of his own in recent times, so Mowbray may be hesitant to put him in for another full 90 minutes just a few days later.

This is an area where Mowbray has very little depth though. Scott Wharton was the only other central defender in the squad on Tuesday, and he has been on a long road back of his own.

It means Carter may start again if Atcheson is out, with Wharton called upon off the bench late on.

Of course, the hope will be that both Alebiosu and Atcheson are fit and ready to go against West Ham this weekend. However, if they aren’t deemed fit, the Blackburn Rovers defenders will have the international break to recover and rest up before the next club fixture.

After Saturday, the next league fixture sees Rovers head to Turf Moor to face rivals Burnley. Mowbray will be determined to have key men Alebiosu and Atcheson available for the fixture.

One potential worry is Atcheson’s call-up to the Northern Ireland squad for the upcoming international fixtures. If he isn’t deemed fit for Saturday, there will be questions over whether he should link up with Michael O’Neill‘s national side as planned.

Nigeria have not announced their squad for their AFCON qualifiers, but Alebiosu would be in a similar predicament if he is included in their ranks.

By Darren Plant | 04 Nov 2026 09:20

Hansi Flick has revealed that Barcelona midfielder Rodri "felt a niggle in his muscles" against Aston Villa.

The Catalan giants recorded an impressive 3-0 victory over the Premier League club at Camp Nou on Tuesday night.

Lamine Yamal and a Raphinha double took Barcelona onto nine points from four Champions League fixtures.

However, there was concern midway through the contest when Rodri failed to return for the second half.

According to Mundo Deportivo, the World Cup winner's withdrawal was due to a hamstring issue.

Speaking to the media after the win on Tuesday night, Flick acknowledged that the former Manchester City star was a doubt for the showdown with Atletico Madrid on Sunday.

He told reporters: "He’s felt a niggle in his muscles, and we’ll have to wait until tomorrow."

Flick hinted that Marc Bernal - who replaced Rodri against Villa - would get the nod versus Atletico if required.

On his midfield options, the German added: "Frenkie’s just come back from injury; he played as a centre-back against Alavés, and Bernal has played very well whenever he’s been on the pitch."

Rodri was surprisingly rested for the Champions League fixture against Paris Saint-Germain last month.

However, despite a late return to training over the summer due to the World Cup and injury, the 30-year-old has racked up the fifth-most minutes for a Barcelona outfield player in La Liga this season.

That has led to suggestions of Rodri being used far too frequently, particularly when considering that he played 90 minutes in a 6-1 win over Alaves on Saturday.

When Bernal is at his disposal, Flick needs to give more consideration to handing regular starts to someone who is deemed to be Rodri's understudy.

#2249. Lewis Ferguson insists Rangers move 'will happen' in big transfer declaration

Lewis Ferguson has revealed he expects to play for Rangers one day as the Scotland star opened up on his future.

The Bologna captain has established himself as one of the leading Scottish players abroad after moving to Italy in 2022, with his performances regularly prompting speculation over what could come next.

But the former Aberdeen midfielder has admitted a move to Ibrox is something he believes will eventually happen.

Asked about the prospect of following in the footsteps of his father and uncle, both former Rangers players, Ferguson said: “Ah, I know... Rangers? Let’s just say that sooner or later, I’ll go there; one day I know it’ll happen. [Gazzetta]

"The rest is about today: we’ve bounced back now, and we have to ride this wave.”

For now, though, Ferguson remains firmly committed to Bologna. The Scotland international revealed he received interest over the summer but ultimately decided to remain in Italy after reflecting on everything the club had given him.

45-cap Rangers star left out for Scotland clash as national boss reveals reason

Rangers learn match officials for Saturday night Premiership clash vs St Mirren

He explained: “I’d taken a bit of time out. I’d had some enquiries, yes, but I wanted to figure out exactly what to do with my life, whether to change direction or carry on as I was.

“Then I thought: Bologna has given me so much. On top of that, the Champions League, the Coppa Italia won and lifted as captain, the Europa League which... if we hadn’t come up against Aston Villa, we’d have reached the final, then the moments of friendship, even the tough times like the injury.

“In short: I took my time and then said just four words: Bologna is my home. And I want to stay here for at least another year.”

Ferguson later reiterated his affection for the Italian club while refusing to put a definitive timescale on his stay. He added: “I’m certainly not thinking about how long I’ll stay, but I’m definitely thinking about how I’ll stay.

"Bologna has been home since day one, I’ll say it again, and I’m having a brilliant time here.”

The midfielder has also embraced the responsibility of captaining Bologna, an achievement he described as a major source of pride as a Scotsman playing abroad.

#2247. Di’Shon Bernard fit but three players still out as Dave Challinor issues Sheffield Wednesday injury latest

Sheffield Wednesday face Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup on Sunday as Dave Challinor takes charge of his new side for the first time. The new SWFC boss has a squad full of talented players to pick from, but he has injuries to consider too.

And, speaking to the press ahead of his maiden game, Challinor confirmed that trio Liam Cooper, Joe Lumley and Ali Al-Hamadi are set to remain out. Cooper is more of a precautionary decision, while Lumley and Al-Hamadi are targeting returns to training next week.

In better news, Di’Shon Bernard is fit and available having trained all week. Challinor and the Owls will remain cautious with his load management after a long time on the sidelines.

Cup matches against opponents in lower divisions often offers teams the chance to rotate, giving fringe players the chance to come in and impress. It will be intriguing to see just what sort of approach Challinor takes though, as he gets to grips with his Sheffield Wednesday squad.

Early on, Challinor has to determine his strongest starting lineup. The most efficient way to do so will be by playing as strong a side as possible in this FA Cup clash with Oldham Athletic.

Alternatively, Challinor will have done his research before taking over at SWFC, and he watched the Blackpool draw. If he already has a good idea of his favoured XI, he can take this game to give chances to those projected to be on the fringes to get a clearer picture of the full squad at his disposal.

It is an interesting dilemma for Challinor, and it will be interesting to see how he approaches it.

Lumley’s continued absence means Ashley Maynard-Brewer will likely keep his place in goal. Cooper being on the sidelines gives Bernard a good chance of coming straight into the side as well.

Challinor had a strong squad at his disposal at Stockport County, but it feels like this Sheffield Wednesday group is a level up. There are experienced pros like Cooper, Barry Bannan and Liam Palmer, alongside players who could arguably be at a higher level, such as Callum Slattery, Billy Mitchell and defender Bernard.

Pair this with the youthful exuberance of Ernie Weaver, Will Grainger and Harry Gray, and SWFC have a squad built for the short and long-term. There’s a good balance of profiles and characters, making up a recipe for success at Hillsborough.

Challinor faces plenty of selection headaches in deciding his best lineup, and he will be excited to find it out.

#2245. PSG: Who is David Boly, the highly sought-after young prospect who scored against Villarreal?

By Axel Clody | 04 Nov 2026 10:08

Paris Saint-Germain cruised to a 6-0 win over Villarreal on Tuesday night in the Champions League. But, beyond the demonstration, the Paris club saw academy graduate David Boly score the final goal of the game.

Introduced in the 74th minute in place of the captain for the night, Achraf Hakimi, David Boly made his mark four minutes later with a goal full of determination: a run into the Villarreal box and a thunderous strike to beat the unfortunate Peter Gulacsi, who had already been beaten five times.

As relayed by L’Equipe, the young right-back became the second-youngest player in Paris Saint-Germain history to score in European competition, at 17 years and 285 days. The record is held by his team-mate Warren Zaire-Emery, who scored against Borussia Dortmund in 2023 at 17 years and... 280 days.

After the game, David Boly expressed his joy at scoring in front of the media: ‘I receive the ball from Joao Neves, I get in front of the goal and I shoot (smiles). I see that it goes in and, well, it was incredible, honestly.’

But behind the celebrations around the win and the academy graduate’s first goal, one question still remains: will David Boly be Achraf Hakimi’s long-term understudy at Paris Saint-Germain?

As relayed by L’Equipe, the 17-year-old Frenchman is still not a professional. The French club have nonetheless offered him a first contract, with ‘a very high financial proposal’, a sign of confidence from the leaders of Ligue 1 and the Champions League, who also say they no longer want to offer ‘oversized’ contracts.

David Boly has not yet signed, with plenty of competition for his signature: big European clubs and ‘more modest’ sides are interested in the talented youngster.

If Paris Saint-Germain want to tie down the defender, like Quentin Ndjantou, compared to a Formula 1 car by Luis Enrique, his playing time in the coming months will influence the decision he takes.

From the player’s side, it can also be argued that a professional contract is simply a way of monetising his talent: David Boly will always leave Paris Saint-Germain for a higher fee with a contract than without one, with his academy deal ending in the summer of 2027.

The Paris side nonetheless seem to be working hard to show the young talent their confidence: playing time, with two appearances, one in Ligue 1 and one in the Champions League, and a high salary for his potential first contract.

According to L’Equipe, David Boly ‘wishes to continue his career at his youth club’. The trend is therefore towards the contract being signed soon.

But when? And, with such exposure in the Champions League, can the right-back’s entourage keep waiting, hoping for a financial bidding war from the many suitors?

If, with his goal and fine introduction, David Boly pushes a little harder to be Achraf Hakimi’s genuine understudy at Paris Saint-Germain, the very status of understudy behind the best right-back in the world could ultimately lead him to ask another question: could the young academy product not be a starter... elsewhere?

#2244. Derby County dealt another injury blow with key forward set for six weeks out

Derby County went another game without a win as they were torn apart by Norwich City 4-0 on Tuesday night. John Eustace is under serious pressure as DCFC host Bolton Wanderers, in what could be his last chance to turn things round at Pride Park.

The Rams have not been helped by injuries this season and they have been dealt yet another blow now. John Eustace has told the BBC that Sammie Szmodics is set for surgery on a knee injury that will see him out for six weeks.

The forward limped off in the defeat to Norwich City during the second-half. Szmodics had been playing through the injury but after aggravating it on Tuesday, surgery was the only option.

He has made a decent start to this season having returned to the club in the summer after an initial loan deal last term.

Losing Szmodics a significant blow for Derby County, particularly given their precarious position in the Championship. Eustace’s side sit 23rd with just seven points from 14 games, having recorded only one win all season.

Szmodics is joint-top scorer with three league goals, alongside Bobby Clark, and has also provided one assist. Eustace described the loss as “huge” after confirming Szmodics will undergo surgery and miss approximately six weeks, potentially ruling him out until mid-December.

DCFC already have several players unavailable. While six weeks might ordinarily be manageable, the Rams’ lack of goals, creativity and squad depth makes this a considerable setback as they attempt to escape the relegation zone.

Filip Bilbija looks the most likely direct replacement for Szmodics in the No.10 role, with Bobby Clark another strong option. Szmodics has predominantly featured as an attacking midfielder in Eustace’s preferred 4-2-3-1 formation, operating behind the striker with freedom to get forward.

Bilbija has already been tipped to fill that position against Bolton, allowing Clark to remain in the double pivot alongside Lewis Travis or Alex Mowatt. Clark is also joint-top scorer with three goals and has the versatility to play further forward if required.

Other alternatives include Rhian Brewster, who can operate centrally or out wide, while Mohammed Fuseini and Corey Blackett-Taylor offer further attacking options. Henrik Meister and Carlton Morris could also provide cover in a supporting role.

Ryan Hedges is another fitness doubt, adding to Derby’s injury concerns. Eustace has options, but none quite replicate Szmodics’ experience and goal threat in the No.10 position.

#2243. Celtic alarm bells ringing as 'never seen before' signs spark Martin O'Neill warning

Robert Snodgrass admits he is seeing worrying signs at Celtic that he has "never seen before" as the pressure cranks up on Martin O'Neill.

The Hoops boss is feeling the heat after managing just three wins in 10 games, and Snodgrass reckons there are reasons for concern.

But it's not only results causing alarm, with Snodgrass fearing Celtic's off-field turmoil has seeped into the dressing room and left O'Neill fighting to keep the wolves from the door.

Speaking to Europa League odds, Snodgrass said: "I'm seeing signs from Celtic that I've never seen before, not just performance-wise but with too much political chatter being made public. Celtic's strength has always been keeping things in-house, backing each other and driving forward together.

Read more: Your 'loud and crazy' supporters won't stop us winning at Celtic Park

"It feels like board-level issues have crept into the squad, making it acceptable, it seems, to speak freely about negative tactical or internal matters. I don't like seeing it. The job at Celtic isn't just winning matches, it's winning titles. That's the standard.

"The main thing is to stop the talking and deliver on the pitch. They have a big game against Besiktas where they need a solid performance.

"Martin O'Neill's teams over the years were always solid. They're still top of the league, which is positive, but the high standards they've set in recent years mean expectations are huge.

"When you set those standards, people come for you, so you have to keep the wolves away from the door. We thought they'd turned a corner by beating Hearts, but losing 2-0 to Falkirk brings the questions back.

"Growing up watching Celtic's success, you know that facing adversity can actually be the making of a team. People feel they've left the door open for Rangers and Hearts instead of driving forward and pulling away.

"Seeing board-level matters reach pitch level is never good because it creeps into the players' minds. It creates a real challenge, but the only way to get everyone off their backs is to stick together and put in winning performances on the pitch.

"Every game at Celtic comes with huge pressure. The demand in Glasgow for both clubs is winning. They're powerhouses who hate losing. There's a specific demand to break down low blocks domestically, which is a different challenge from Europe.

"Celtic taking their domestic approach into Europe has been difficult over the last few years. Playing three games in seven days comes fast, so you have to rotate and manage the squad effectively rather than getting bogged down in off-field noise.

"You see Martin O'Neill using his experience to keep the players united through defeats because the games come thick and fast. Europe has been a test, but domestically, winning trophies is the absolute expectation."

#2242. Cardiff vs. West Ham: Will Scott Parker be in dugout for Championship game after Hammers return?

West Ham United have announced the appointment of Scott Parker as their new head coach.

On Tuesday morning, the Hammers revealed that they had parted company with Nuno Espirito Santo.

That was despite the club sitting just six points adrift of leaders Middlesbrough in the Championship table, and a game at Cardiff City taking place on Wednesday night.

However, given the speed that reports emerged over an approach for Parker, it soon became apparent that the wheels over a replacement had already been in motion.

Just over 24 hours since Nuno was sacked, the return of former West Ham midfielder Parker has been confirmed.

West Ham have revealed that the 46-year-old will be in the dugout for the game with Cardiff.

Parker told the club's official website: "It is a very proud moment for me to step into this role and return to a Club that means so much to me and my family. A lot of very fond memories of my time here - four years of probably the best football I played in my career.

"There was a warmth, a love, a feeling that I probably didn’t have anywhere else. There was something which stood out constantly and that was the relationship that I built up with the fanbase here. That rapport and respect, that I felt instantly.

"More than anything, this is an opportunity that I am very excited about. I feel privileged and honoured to be the guy that wants to lead us back to the Premier League and be the Head Coach of this Football Club at this moment in time.

"My energy levels, my enthusiasm, my desire, my passion - it’s absolutely running through and that's the feeling I had as soon as there was a conversation that this was possible. That's exactly why I'm here and why I want to be here.

"From my first calls with Karim [Virani] and Daniel [Křetínský], it was clear they have a shared vision and desire to return the Club to the Premier League as quickly as possible. I share that ambition and I’m very confident we can achieve it.

"I’ve been in this division three times and I've got out of this division three times. This is a tough division, there is absolutely no denying it. And I will use every ounce of my experience, every ounce of my knowledge, to have an understanding of what we're going to need to bring consistency.

"And that's going to be the key word for all of us really. The consistency of what we do and the commitment levels that we go to will give us the best opportunity to get out of this division and be the team that we can be."

While there has been occasional criticism of Parker's playing style, he boasts an incredible record as a manager in the Championship.

He has guided each of Fulham, Bournemouth and Burnley to promotion to the Premier League.

A total of 76 wins, 41 draws and 21 defeats have been recorded in 138 games in the second tier of English football.

#2241. Real Madrid welcomes sporting royalty as tennis icon joins board of directors

Tennis star Rafael Nadal has become a director of Real Madrid.

The club’s board met on Wednesday and agreed to submit to the general assembly of delegate members ratification of Nadal’s appointment as a new member of the board.

Nadal won 22 grand slam singles titles in a stellar career, including a record 14 French Open crowns. He retired from the sport in 2024.

A club statement described Nadal as “one of the greatest legends in the history of sport and one of the most admired and recognised Spanish athletes in the world”.

The statement added: “For Real Madrid and for all Madridismo it is a great pride that a figure of his sporting and human dimension, a benchmark in Spanish and world sport, becomes part of our board of directors and contributes, from this responsibility, to the present and future of our club.”

Real’s statement continued: “With his extraordinary track record, with 92 individual titles, including 22 grand slams, two Olympic gold medals, five Davis Cups with Spain and number one in the world ranking, Rafael Nadal has starred in a legendary sporting career.

“But his figure represents much more than his successes: his talent, his spirit of permanent improvement, his capacity for sacrifice, his competitiveness, his humility, his respect for rivals and his team-mates, and his commitment to solidarity make him an example on and off the courts.

“All these values are part of Real Madrid’s identity, and Rafael Nadal has represented and defended them throughout his sporting life, becoming a reference for millions of people around the world.”

Nadal has spoken about his love of Real Madrid since childhood in previous interviews, despite his uncle Miguel Angel Nadal having played for their bitter rivals Barcelona.

Nadal has held honorary membership of Real for years, regularly attending matches at the Bernabeu. He was a guest of honour at the 2022 Champions League final in Paris when Real won their 14th Champions League title.

#2240. Swansea City receive significant boost as Vitor Matos issues injury update on key defender

Swansea City have hit a poor run of form after a phenomenal start to the season. The Swans were top of the pile after 12 games, but a run of three defeats in four games has seen them drop to 4th, with a midweek defeat to Portsmouth the latest in that run.

During that Pompey clash, summer signing Stephen Welsh went off with a knee problem that appeared serious. However, as they head to Birmingham City this weekend, Vitor Matos has revealed that the injury is not as bad as first thought and he could be in contention to face Chris Davies’ side, as reported by Wales Online.

Matos said: “We will still assess if he will be available for Saturday but it’s not a bad injury. It’s just a knock that is really really painful.”

Welsh has quickly established himself as one of Swansea City’s most important players since arriving on a free transfer from Celtic in summer 2026. The 26-year-old signed a two-year contract, with the option of a further year, becoming Vitor Matos’ first summer recruit.

Cameron Burgess’ long-term Achilles injury opened the door for Welsh, who has since made the centre-back position his own. He has started 13 of Swansea’s 14 Championship matches, primarily alongside Ben Cabango, and has impressed with his composure, defensive work and ability to play out from the back.

His passing accuracy, progressive distribution and contribution to Swansea’s defensive organisation have made him an ideal fit for Matos’ possession-based approach. His performances have also earned him his first senior Scotland caps.

With Swansea challenging near the top of the table, Welsh has become a key figure. Securing an experienced defender of his quality on a free transfer is proving to be excellent summer business.

Swansea are short of centre-back options without Welsh, although their defensive depth remains manageable in the short term.

Cabango is the obvious leader, while Jenson Seelt offers the most natural replacement after already featuring under Matos. The Sunderland loanee would be the likely partner for Cabango if Welsh is unavailable.

Filip Lissah provides further cover, having demonstrated his versatility by operating centrally, but regularly relying on him could weaken the right-back position. Cameron Burgess remains sidelined with a long-term Achilles injury, leaving Swansea more exposed if Welsh faces an extended absence.

#2239. Barcelona transfer news: Deco tracks four young centre-backs with Real Madrid and Premier League rivals

By Axel Clody | 04 Nov 2026 10:44

Barcelona are already preparing for their next transfer window with one priority: strengthening their central defence. Sporting director Deco is monitoring four young centre-backs.

The situation is starting to become worrying for Hansi Flick. Andreas Christensen has been out since September with a left thigh muscle injury. Jules Kounde and Eric Garcia are also unavailable, while youngster Xavi Espart must observe a long period of rest after his ankle injury.

Against Alaves, the German manager even had to use Frenkie de Jong at centre-back, alongside Pau Cubarsi.

Although not all of these absences are expected to last, they highlight the fragility of the Barcelona squad. Kounde’s situation is also worth monitoring in the medium term, as his future at right-back is not entirely separate from the club’s thinking on recruitment.

In this context, Deco is looking in particular for a left-footed defender, comfortable in build-up play and capable of defending far from his own goal to complement Flick’s very high defensive line.

According to Sport, three new young players feature in the early thinking of Barcelona’s sporting director.

The first is Vitor Reis. The 20-year-old Brazilian defender belongs to Manchester City and already has European experience, having spent a loan spell at Girona. His athletic profile and technical quality match Barcelona’s requirements. However, it remains to be seen whether he is the ideal choice, as his playing time is very limited, with four appearances and 199 minutes.

Another option from Brazil is Arthur Dias, who plays for Athletico Paranaense. The 19-year-old defender is more of a bet on potential, with the possibility of gradually joining the Catalan club’s rotation. Finally,

Ruud Nijstad of FC Twente completes this first list. The 18-year-old Dutchman is considered a defender with high potential and is also targeted ny Manchester United and Bayern Munich. His room for progression makes him an interesting target for a club that also wants to prepare for the future.

Finally, the name of Youri Baas particularly catches the attention of the Catalan club’s decision-makers. At 23, the Ajax defender already has more experience at the highest level than the other three targets.

Left-footed, strong in duels and able to play at left-back, the Dutchman offers appreciable versatility. His quality in build-up play is also an argument for fitting into Flick’s system.

Under contract until June 2028, Baas will not necessarily be easy to prise away from Amsterdam. His value has risen and several English and German clubs are also following his performances. Real Madrid have also shown an interest in the Dutchman, according to Fichajes, although that option is less current than the one involving Barcelona.

For Deco, the equation will therefore be as much financial as sporting. The salary cap limits Barcelona’s options and could force the club to consider a departure before recruiting. Baas nevertheless appears a particularly coherent option to meet an immediate need, without abandoning a recruitment policy focused on young players.

#2238. Wow' - Wolves star Adam Armstrong drops reaction after big personal moment

Wolverhampton Wanderers striker Adam Armstrong has taken to social media after recording his 500th career appearance in his side's 2-1 victory over Bristol City at Ashton Gate on Wednesday night.

The experienced striker has played for a plethora of clubs in the Premier League, Championship and League One, but for now is fully focused on aiding Wolves' aim of an instant return to the top-flight under Cesar Peixoto.

Armstrong played 85 of the 90 minutes in the West Country before being replaced by Tawanda Chirewa, just three minutes after his initial effort, which was saved by Brad Collins, was turned home on the rebound by Newcastle United loanee, Sean Neave, who went on to net the winner in the final minute.

It was a joyous occasion for all associated with the Black Country outfit, as a fourth win in five matches saw them climb into the second tier's automatic promotion places, just one point behind leaders Middlesbrough ahead of Saturday's trip to Wrexham.

Armstrong signed for his current employers in January from Southampton in a deal reportedly worth £7m plus £2m in add-ons.

The experienced forward has made 15 of his 500 appearances in the early weeks of the current campaign, during which he has scored four times, and will be hoping it ends in similar fashion to the 2023/24 season when he tasted promotion under Russell Martin at St Mary's.

Taking to Instagram after noting his personal milestone, the North East native said: "Wow what a night and what a win!!

"Who would have thought, 500. To the fans, teammates, staff at all the clubs. You played a huge part in me getting to this milestone.

"To my family. Thanks for all the support along the way. You are what drives me each day. Next goal promotion and 600 games," he added.

Among those in the replies paying tribute to the longevity of the striker's career were Ben Brereton Diaz, Will Smallbone, Harry Wilson, Bradley Dack and his current Molineux teammates, Mateus Mane and Tommy Doyle.

The Newcastle United academy graduate has enjoyed a fine first 500 games, with 102 of those coming in the Premier League, albeit he would have ultimately liked to have seen a better return than eight goals and nine assists in the top-flight.

However, Armstrong's clinical ability in front of goal hasn't ever been disputed in the Championship for the likes of Wolves, Southampton and Blackburn Rovers in particular.

Where does Adam Armstrong rank among the Championship's best strikers? Comment below!

289 of his outings have come in the division which he currently plies his trade in, and so have 94 of his goals and 37 of his assists, none bigger than his winner for Saints in the 2024 play-off final against Leeds United at Wembley Stadium.

Armstrong has started in nine of his 12 Championship outings so far this season, but it's fair to say the striker hadn't had the greatest of starts to the new season until recently.

He has since scored two in Wolves' past three games whilst having a big role in Neave's initial equaliser midweek, and this comes after receiving plenty of backing from Peixoto to start firing plenty of goals in.

The Newcastle United loanee's late brace ensured a vital three points for the Old Gold

“It is important for his confidence but he is an experienced player and I don’t believe he was losing confidence," said the Portuguese.

With Armstrong setting his dream out in terms of promotion, he will hope to start his next century of games with a positive result in North Wales this weekend.

#2236. Everton vs. Coventry City: Head-to-head record and past meetings ahead of Premier League clash

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 10:47, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 10:48

Everton will welcome Coventry City to the Hill Dickinson Stadium in the Premier League on Friday night.

The Toffees are currently seventh in the Premier League table, boasting 13 points from nine matches, while Coventry are currently 16th in the division.

Here, Sports Mole takes an in-depth look at the head-to-head record and previous meetings between the two sides ahead of their contest in Gameweek 10.

Everton and Coventry have locked horns on 80 previous occasions, with the former leading the overall head-to-head record 37 wins to the latter's 23, while there have also been 20 draws.

This is a fixture that dates back to March 1910, when Everton beat Coventry 2-0 in the FA Cup.

Fast forward to the present day, the last meeting between the two teams came in the Premier League in January 2001, with Everton posting a 3-1 success over Coventry.

However, Coventry have actually won two of their last three meetings, including a 2-1 victory when the pair last locked horns at the home of Everton in December 2000.

There have been 18 previous Premier League meetings between Everton and Coventry, with the latter posting six wins to the latter's three, while there have also been nine draws.

Everton have only won one home Premier League match against Coventry - a 2-0 success at Goodison Park back in April 1999.

#2235. Supercomputer names the 3 EFL League One clubs that will get promoted

We're a quarter of the way through the EFL season, which gives us a good sample size to set more informed predictions about how the campaign will play out in all three divisions.

In League One, Huddersfield Town and Stockport County have been the two sides who have consistently impressed and deservedly led the way in the top two.

But below them, Luton Town have picked up a head of steam and some strong underlying numbers are starting to translate into results. They even took a lead at the Accu Stadium on Tuesday night against the league leaders, but were forced to settle for a draw.

Then, further down the table, Sheffield Wednesday have appointed Dave Challinor, and many believe that's the start of a resurgence at Hillsborough. Leicester City have the quality on the pitch and a system that's starting to settle under Russell Martin, and between them, the likes of Reading, Plymouth Argyle and Oxford United will be competitive, too.

So, it's all shaping up to be a very intriguing promotion race with a lot of sides who will believe they can go up, but who does AI think will be in the Championship next season? Football League World has taken a look to find out.

Winning the league, quite surprisingly in AI's eyes, are Oxford. The surprise mainly comes from the Yellows being nowhere near the top six in many critics' pre-season predictions.

There was logic to that reason, too, as Oxford weren't able to spend money and invest in the squad over the summer due to an embargo imposed on them after failing to comply with the EFL's SCMP regulations.

Football League World takes an in-depth look at the TEN most frustrating EFL Championship players right now

The Yellows were also going into the season with a novice head coach, too, after Matt Bloomfield was sacked in favour of ex-Arsenal and Wales midfielder Aaron Ramsey. Quite bluntly, no one really knew what to expect.

However, Ramsey has more than hit the ground running in the dugout, and Oxford's embargo, despite its frustrations, did mean that the vast majority of their squad that finished 22nd in the Championship and had a mid-table points tally under Bloomfield stuck around. The likes of Mark Harris, Michal Helik and Ciaron Brown have been great.

But also, Ramsey has done well to re-integrate some players many fans felt would never wear an Oxford shirt again. Siriki Dembele has been the main example of that, but Gatlin O'Donkor and Louie Sibley have contributed well, too.

There are questions about the overall depth at the Kassam. Teenagers Mo Missanga and Leo Snowdon are seeing more matchday squads than perhaps expected; albeit Missanga has been brilliant, but hopefully the embargo can be lifted in January, and Ramsey can bring in some reinforcements to help solidify what AI expects to be a title charge.

The current league leaders, Huddersfield, have been forecast by AI to slip from the summit but still secure automatic promotion.

That may be a disappointment right now, but given that the Terriers have failed to even finish in the top six in their two campaigns at third-tier level since their relegation from the Championship, it's a position they should more than take.

There's a clear argument that Huddersfield's squad is already Championship quality. It definitely is at the top end of the pitch. Joe Taylor and Derensili Sanches Fernandes already have 25 goals and assists between them through 14 games, which is quite frankly ridiculous.

But behind them, Cameron Humphreys has been one of the best midfielders in the division for a couple of years now, and it's mental that Huddersfield were able to keep him around for a second loan spell from Ipswich Town, and Archie Collins is more than thriving at a top-end League One club after leaving Peterborough United.

It says a lot that there are question marks over where Ryan Ledson fits in when he comes back from suspension, with the Terriers' club captain and someone with close to 200 Championship appearances patiently waiting on the sidelines.

And it's all being overseen by Martin Drury, an exciting, young manager whose attention to detail and demanding nature of a good performance can ruffle some feathers in the 'only results matter' camp, but it's more than working so far.

There may be a slight drop-off, but one that AI believes shouldn't be too detrimental to their promotion push.

After failing in the play-offs for the last two seasons, AI thinks that Stockport will finally get over the line and secure a second-tier return for the first time since the 2001/02 campaign.

There were some understandable concerns about the immediate future of the Hatters heading into this season, with influential CEO Simon Wilson and manager Dave Challinor moving on after the play-off final, but those have been dispelled very quickly.

There's an argument to be made that County are even better now under Jimmy McNulty than they were under Challinor, and that's no slight on the good work done at Edgeley Park over the previous five years.

They have so many different avenues of attack, whether that be patient, on the floor, working through the lines with Ollie Norwood and a reintegrated Lewis Bate in the pivots, or they can go long and hit Kyle Wootton, who remains one of the best target men strikers in the entire EFL, given how well he can hold the ball up and be a goal threat, too.

Do you agree with AI's promotion predictions in League One this season? Let us know in the comments.

Stockport can sometimes get caught out defensively. It took them a little while to get to grips with their new out-of-possession play at the start of the season, and Oxford did showcase how to beat their high line, once they finally stayed onside.

But any worries of them fading away after back-to-back third-placed finishes seem to have dissipated already, and AI feels that it could be third time's the charm in terms of them earning promotion, winning ahead of the likes of Plymouth, Reading, Sheffield Wednesday, Luton and Leicester.

#2231. Middlesbrough fans think Kim Hellberg got one thing wrong v Wrexham

Middlesbrough fell to their second defeat of the Championship season on Wednesday night, as Wrexham bounced back from consecutive defeats to pick up a vital three points at the Racecourse Ground.

In a game with very few chances, Phil Parkinson's side broke the deadlock with 20 minutes to go through George Thomason, and made sure the points were safe after Ollie Rathbone scored a second six minutes from time.

It's the first time that Middlesbrough have failed to score all season. Kim Hellberg's side have been electric going forward, led primarily by Will Lankshear, and it wasn't a coincidence that his absence after suffering a muscle issue led to Boro being a little less dangerous in front of goal.

However, it wasn't just the fact that Middlesbrough lined up with the likes of Kyle Joseph, Tommy Conway and David Strelec as the central nine at times on Wednesday night which frustrated Boro fans, as for the first time in quite a while, Hellberg himself wasn't without criticism.

Kim Hellberg is one of the more tactically fluid head coaches in the Championship today. He's comfortable deploying a back four or a back three, and spoke in his press conference last week about being happy operating with two strikers.

Unfortunately, the switch to a back three in possession and a back five out of it against Wrexham on Wednesday night has drawn quite a bit of criticism from Boro fans.

With Hellberg's side having a chance to extend their lead atop the table to four points last night against a Wrexham side who have underwhelmed this season and had lost their last two, the supporters were disappointed to see a perceived 'negative' lineup.

"Far too negative against a bang average side Kim. I hope you've learned from this? You know the score mate, sort it out. UTB!🇦🇹" one fan said on X in response to the Swede's post-match interview.

"5 at the back? More defensive against Moore? A team sat in the bottom half of the league, shambles Kim," another said.

Do you think Kim Hellberg got it wrong against Wrexham on Wednesday? Let us know in the comments.

Other fans accused Hellberg of "inviting Wrexham to give us a game," while others expressed frustration at setting up in a five "against a poor team." "He has to take responsibility for the formation and selections!" was also among the comments.

"Lost for words Kim? Poor team selection and formation tonight. Never play with a back 3 again," was said bluntly. Evidently, the majority of Middlesbrough fans are hoping Hellberg reverts to a back four on Saturday at Watford.

Ultimately, while Wrexham haven't enjoyed the start to the season that many expected they would have, Phil Parkinson's side is still full of talented players who can hurt any team on a good day.

This one can be chalked up to a sub-par performance from a Middlesbrough side and a bit of a better one from a Wrexham perspective, and the fact that Boro are still sitting top of the division regardless, just shows how strong they have been to start the season.

No league champion goes the whole campaign without losing, and those at the Riverside will be hoping that this is just a mere bump in the road en route to a Premier League return.

Kim Hellberg's side are in action at Vicarage Road on Saturday as they take on Watford in the final game before the November international break.

#2230. Crystal Palace vs. Hoffenheim: How to watch Europa League clash, date, time, live stream, TV channel

By Calum Burrowes | 04 Nov 2026 11:15, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 11:19

Gameweek four in the Europa League sees Crystal Palace welcome Hoffenheim to Selhurst Park for a league-phase clash this Thursday.

The two teams are meeting for the first time in a competitive fixture, with both looking to strengthen their chances of progressing to the knockout rounds, and here, Sports Mole provides details of how to tune into the match.

Crystal Palace vs. Hoffenheim will kick off at 17:45 UK time on Thursday evening.

The match is one of several Europa League fixtures taking place on the same evening, with Bournemouth also in action away to Sparta Prague while Sunderland welcome Dinamo Zagreb.

This Europa League match will take place at Crystal Palace's home ground, Selhurst Park, which has a capacity of 25,194.

The Eagles will be looking to make the most of home advantage, having opened their European campaign with a convincing 4-0 victory over Lech Poznan earlier this season.

Crystal Palace's clash against the German side will be shown on TNT Sports 1 for viewers in the UK, with coverage also available on TNT Sports Ultimate.

Fans with access to TNT Sports can stream the match through HBO Max provided their subscription includes the relevant sports coverage.

Match highlights of Crystal Palace's encounter against Christian Ilzer's side will be available through TNT Sports' digital platforms and YouTube channel, while both clubs are also expected to share footage and reaction through their official channels.

Crystal Palace are looking to bounce back from a difficult spell in the Premier League, where three successive defeats have left Pierre Sage's side in the relegation zone after nine matches.

The Eagles have collected four points from their opening three Europa League fixtures, beginning with a 4-0 home victory over Lech Poznan before losing 3-2 away to Lyon and drawing 2-2 with Besiktas.

They currently sit 15th in the 36-team League Phase table and will be keen to strengthen their position with another home win.

Sage's side can take encouragement from their record at Selhurst Park, where they are unbeaten in seven European matches, winning five and drawing two.

However, they will need to improve their recent domestic form if their new manager is to avoid coming under further pressure.

As for Hoffenheim, they have made a less convincing start to their European campaign, collecting three points from their first three matches.

Defeats to OFI Crete and Besiktas were followed by an impressive and much-needed 3-0 victory over Lyon, which lifted Ilzer's side to 23rd in the standings.

Die Kraichgauer have struggled to maintain that momentum, though, exiting the DFB-Pokal on penalties to Holstein Kiel before suffering a 2-0 Bundesliga defeat against Werder Bremen last weekend.

Hoffenheim are also aiming to improve their away record in Europe, having won just one of their last six continental away matches, while they have lost all five of their previous meetings with English opposition.

Former Cardiff City cult hero Michael Chopra has issued a claim on the form of Jack Moylan, whose fine start to life at the Cardiff City Stadium continued with a first-half hat-trick in the 3-3 draw with West Ham United.

Much of the pre-match discourse in South Wales was on Scott Parker's first game in charge of the Hammers, but it seemed as though the Bluebirds were going to make it three successive losses for them through Moylan's individual brilliance.

The Republic of Ireland international opened the scoring with a stunning strike from distance after 11 minutes, before netting twice more before the interval.

After drawing a foul from Morato, Moylan beat Mads Hermansen from the penalty spot before glancing home his hat-trick and second in as many minutes just six minutes before half-time.

Ultimately, West Ham staged a thrilling fightback and were level on 82 minutes through goals from Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman, but that took nothing away from the stunning form Moylan continues to demonstrate in South Wales.

Who are the most clutch players in the Championship that could whip in a last-minute set piece worldie?

Moylan arrived in the Welsh capital in August from fellow Championship newcomers, Lincoln City, despite only signing fresh terms at the ProAmpac Stadium six months ago.

After scoring 11 times in 31 League One appearances for last season's third tier winners, the attacking midfielder is already the joint-second-highest goal-getter in the Championship, with his treble against the Irons seeing him match Andri Gudjohnsen's tally of nine for Blackburn Rovers.

The six-time international has posted three further assists as Cardiff sit 18th on 15 points, and Chopra, who scored 63 times in 159 appearances, had this to say on the 25-year-old.

"Said it a couple months ago @JackMoylan8 is some player," the former striker posted on X. "A first half hat trick unbelievable.

Another responded: "No it isn’t mate cmon ! Only been here a couple months! End of season maybe but not January!"

However, not all were of this opinion, with another social media comment reading: "January is going to be a chance to see how the board see us when the bids for Moylan come in."

"Difference between Cardiff pushing for top half and being dragged into a relegation battle. Nobody else in that team has that level of quality in front of goal," was another verdict of Moylan's immediate impact.

Given Chopra's cult hero status at Cardiff, it was high praise that one Cardiff fan wrote: "Nearly, but not quite, as good as you!," in response to the former striker's opinion.

Cardiff fans, do you fear a sudden January exit for Jack Moylan? Comment below!

Moylan's performances have also been noted by supporters of other clubs, with a Birmingham City fan adding: "No chance he doesn’t get a prem move in January, bloke is class."

Few would be surprised if Moylan's current form perks the interest of clubs ahead of the winter window, although Cardiff fans will be hoping they avoid a quick sale of the playmaker.

However, the Bluebirds would still hold significant power in negotiations after agreeing a four-year deal with the Dubliner, whilst those associated with Lincoln are hoping that a sell-on clause was inserted in his sale.
