#2227. Barella admits Inter have changed derby approach in recent years: “We’re too inconsistent”

After the game, Nicolo Barella attempted to lessen the damage of last night’s loss to AC Milan in his post-game press conference.

For Milan, last night was another instance in what is becoming a new norm. After Inter’s streak of derby wins, it’s now the Rossoneri who are enjoying themselves. They’ve won each of their last four meetings and haven’t lost in eight games.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

In short, despite all of the managerial changes that have taken place, the Diavolo remain on top when it comes to playing the Nerazzurri.

Of course, last night’s win was also monumental considering what it meant for the Serie A table. Rather than falling 10 points behind, they only sit four points behind Inter and Lazio, who share first.

Speaking at a post-match conference, Barella admitted that Inter are going through a bad cycle in the derbies, but was keen to remember the streak from a few years ago, as MilanNews relayed.

“Certainly, if we talk about this, we’ve been lacking the ideal approach to matches since the start of the year. Right now, it’s a clear criticality for us.

“It’s true that we’ve shown our strength on many occasions, perhaps coming out on top in the second half or whenever we manage to break the deadlock, but it’s clear that not every match goes that way.

“We can’t expect to always repeat the same pattern, especially when we face a packed schedule with matches every three days. Fatigue, both physical and mental, is starting to take its toll, and we need to learn to manage matches better without being forced to keep them in the balance until the ninetieth minute.”

Is there a feeling that, whatever you do, Milan will prevail in derbies?

“Let’s say I’ve also experienced it the other way around. I’ve had a lot of derby wins in a row, so I don’t know if it’s a cyclical thing, but it’s definitely a game we haven’t approached in the best way in recent years, recently.

“There have been derbies where we’ve played well and lost, where we’ve played poorly and maybe drawn, but we’re too inconsistent in derbies, and right now, even in important matches. Even though we won, we still needed to do better.”

Do you still have the fierce desire to prove that you are the strongest?

“Being the strongest is sometimes not enough, because we’ve also been the strongest team, or the one everyone thought was the strongest, but then we didn’t win. So right now, I think we need to focus on something else rather than our strength.

“It’s true that if we look back at the last few games, many goals come from individual errors, and we need to improve on those, each of us, because unfortunately, individual errors work against the team. But as a team, we need to improve a bit in terms of solidity, which is what has always set us apart, what got us to the Champions League final, what helped us win the Scudetto, and perhaps right now we’re not the best version of ourselves. So we need to work on that. We still have the desire to win, for sure, because that’s what keeps us going to the pitch every day and motivates us, but we need to improve.”

#2226. Spurs will be surprised but Leicester's won't by Oliver Skipp at Charlton

Having shone when coming through the academy at Tottenham Hotspur and then experienced a bright start to his career in the EFL, Oliver Skipp is again struggling this season, now at Charlton Athletic after a tough time of it at Leicester City.

Following their promotion back to the Championship via the 2024-25 League One play-offs, Charlton have looked to assert themselves as a part of the furniture again in the second-tier, under the management of Nathan Jones.

A young squad under Jones as they gained promotion, Charlton did have a fair bit of experience dotted around their squad but, as ever with a team heading into a campaign seeking to avoid ‘second season syndrome’, they have again sought to add more pedigree to their squad.

That may well have even been with a view to kicking on into the middle-of-the-table and, albeit as an outsider, looked at the top eight and the expanded play-off spots to eight teams in the Championship this season.

One player that was brought in with good pedigree to provide extra quality in the middle of the park was Oliver Skipp – but the 26-year-old is enduring another struggle this season down at The Valley.

Over the summer, Charlton added a fair bit of experience to the middle of the park with the signing of Nathaniel Chalobah on a free transfer from Sheffield Wednesday and Timothy Ouma from Slavia Prague.

Both defensive midfielders, it was somewhat surprising that The Addicks then signed Oliver Skipp on loan from Leicester City on the final day of the campaign – albeit in a Nathan Jones-coached side, perhaps you can’t have too many conservative and defensive-minded midfielders.

Skipp, Ouma and Chalobah joined a squad that already boasted other key midfielders such as Conor Coventry, Greg Docherty and the slightly more advanced Sonny Carey, while the versatile Joe Rankin-Costello is also capable of playing in a central midfield role.

Whether it be a 4-2-3-1 system or even a three-at-the-back with either one or two so-called ‘number tens’; there tends to only be room for two out and out box-to-box or defensive central midfielders – and with the quality Skipp has previously shown and the experience he already has in his mid-20’s; Skipp was viewed as a floor-raising addition to a Charlton side that had won their opening three games of the campaign before his arrival.

However, Charlton have since gone on a ten-match winless run of form after the arrival of Skipp and his own individual performance have perhaps been reflective of the malaise and regression of the team as a whole.

A look at seven clubs who should consider trying to sign former Tottenham Hotspur and England man Dele Alli.

The former England U21 international came into the side immediately and played the full 90 minutes as Charlton endured their first loss of the season – a 4-0 hammering at the hands of Stoke City.

He has gone on to start seven of the next eight matches, too, only not involved against Norwich City after being hooked at half-time in a 2-0 defeat to Watford a week earlier.

He came back into the eleven against Middlesbrough but found himself in a midfield that was completely overrun by the league leaders in a 3-0 defeat at the Riverside Stadium in that one.

Keeping out Coventry in midfield, as well as even Chalobah, who had started life in South London well, being moved to the periphery of things seems now to be a surprising inevitability for Skipp.

Once a metronomic figure in terms of his passing and tempo setting when coming through Tottenham youth teams and then on loan at Norwich; Skipp has badly struggled to adapt to the more conservative nature of Jones’ style.

Skipp has remained tidy enough on the ball with an 83.1% pass success rate but he is averaging too few passes per game and allowing games to pass him by, averaging more than 12 passes per 90 fewer than Conor Coventry, as an example.

Previously better known for his ability on the ball, it is his defensive work that has been his more impressive suit thus far this season but, with ball-winners such as Chalobah at the club, more quality was to be expected of the Leicester loanee.

That quality was shown when coming through at Spurs as he made the fringes of the first-team picture in North London and even began to emerge with more regular minutes when coached by Antonio Conte – who he once suggested he could help become a ‘top midfielder’.

His breakthrough into more regular minutes at Spurs had come after an extremely impressive loan stint at Norwich during the 2020-21 campaign – named in the PFA Team of the Year for the second division that season as The Canaries won the Championship title under Daniel Farke.

That season, Skipp was that metronome in Norwich’s proactive and entertaining side, averaging 51.5 passes per game with an 88% pass success rate – dwarfing his figures at Charlton so far this season and highlighting his drop off.

That drop off being seen by Charlton supporters this season may come as a surprise to Spurs and Norwich supporters but it won’t be much of a shock for Leicester fans.

Purchased by The Foxes in the summer of 2024 for a fee believed to be in the region of £20 million, following Leicester’s Championship title-winning campaign under Enzo Maresca, Skipp linked up with Steve Cooper at the King Power Stadium.

A good coach but a pragmatic one, the passing and ability to retain possession that had been qualities of Skipp weren’t necessarily going to be used too much for a Cooper-led relegation battler.

However, his fierceness in the tackle and general industry also diminished in a Leicester squad that succumbed to back-to-back relegations from the Premier League and into League One.

Perhaps a very good example of Leicester’s issues in the last half a decade, being a big money signing brought in without too much sense in terms of exactly what they wanted; Skipp badly struggled at the King Power Stadium.

Having been one of the best players in the Championship and then someone seemingly coming close to England recognition in the years before his arrival at Leicester; a loss of confidence and inability to rediscover his quality on the ball led to Skipp sticking out as a fairly hapless figure in Leicester’s badly underperforming side.

Brought in for such a big fee, he managed just ten starts in his debut campaign as they were relegated from the Premier League before being a peripheral figure in the opening few months of last season.

Skipp became a key man for a Leicester side that became enemies of the fanbase and his failure to rediscover any sort of form at Charlton thus far will not come as much of a surprise to Foxes supporters.

#2224. Everton vs Coventry City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 11:20, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 21:36

Everton will be bidding to make it three straight wins in all competitions when they welcome Coventry City to the Hill Dickinson Stadium on Friday night.

The Toffees are currently seventh in the Premier League table, only two points behind fifth-placed Liverpool, while Coventry occupy 16th spot in the division.

Everton boast a record of three wins, four draws and two defeats from their nine league matches this season, which represents a strong start to the campaign for the Toffees given the criticism that came their way due to an underwhelming summer transfer window.

David Moyes' side sit seventh in the division and will enter Friday's match off the back of a 1-0 victory over Newcastle United at St James' Park.

Everton also beat the Magpies in the EFL Cup last Thursday, so it has been a strong week for the Merseyside outfit, who will be eyeing a third straight victory on Friday night.

The Toffees will face Brentford in their first match after the November international break before the small matter of a clash with Liverpool at the end of the month.

Everton have incredibly only won one home Premier League match against Coventry - a 2-0 success at Goodison Park back in April 1999, although they do lead the overall head-to-head record between the two teams 37 wins to Coventry's 23.

Coventry have actually won two of their last three games against Everton, including a 2-1 victory when the pair last locked horns at the home of the Toffees in December 2000.

The Sky Blues lost their opening four Premier League matches of the season, but they have now been victorious in three of their last five during an impressive run of form, overcoming Nottingham Forest, Newcastle United and Sunderland.

Frank Lampard's side beat Sunderland 3-1 last time out, and they currently sit 16th in the division on nine points, only three points behind eighth-placed Bournemouth.

Coventry are out of the EFL Cup, so their focus until January at least will be purely on the Premier League, and they will face Crystal Palace and Leeds United in their two matches following the November international break.

The Sky Blues have only lost three of their 18 Premier League meetings against Everton, and their recent form will certainly hand them confidence ahead of this contest.

Everton will again be missing Jack Grealish, with the on-loan Manchester City attacker set to be absent for the third game in a row due to a disciplinary issue.

It would not be a surprise to see the same XI from the Newcastle clash last time out given the impressive nature of the performance and result.

However, a late check needs to be made on Jarrad Branthwaite, who was substituted in the latter stages of the clash with Newcastle.

Thierno Barry has scored eight goals in 12 appearances this season and will continue in the final third of the field, while Kiernan Dewsbury-Hall should again feature as the number 10.

As for Coventry, Aurele Amenda (calf), Haji Wright (thigh), Josh Eccles (ankle) and Luke Woolfenden (knee) are out of the Premier League match.

Meanwhile, Bobby Thomas (calf) and Frank Onyeka (muscle) both need to be assessed.

Jack Rudoni is the team's leading goalscorer this season with four, and the 25-year-old will again feature as the number 10; Taiwo Awoniyi is also set to continue in the side.

Some clubs mean more to some players, but it’s not a one-way street. Indeed, some players mean more to some clubs and Milan share that relationship with Kaka. The Brazilian great enjoyed two spells at San Siro, playing 223 times and scoring 77 goals.

Blues have won four of their last five Championship matches and sit third in the table ahead of the final round of fixtures before the two-week international break, with Swansea only two places below on goal difference.

Davies isn't exactly short of options at this moment in time, although James Beadle and August Priske were omitted from the matchday squad against Millwall through injury.

Paik had been a mainstay in Davies' side for much of his tenure, having arrived just months before the 41-year-old was appointed two years ago, signing from Jeonbuk Hyundai Motors in January 2024.

2026 has been a tale of frustration for the South Korean though. Having missed a portion of last season with injury misfortune, he hasn't been able to aid Birmingham's impressive start this time around for similar reasons.

The 30-time South Korean international has featured 10 times in all competitions this season, albeit only four of his eight Championship outings have come from the starting lineup.

The last of those came through a seven-minute cameo in the 2-2 draw with Middlesbrough before the extended first international break of the season.

Paik has missed the past seven Championship games after the South Korean Football Association released a statement on the decision to return him to the West Midlands club for "protection."

Earlier this week, Davies revealed the midfielder could be involved in some capacity, and images have now surfaced of the 29-year-old partaking in full training with his teammates ahead of the pivotal weekend showdown.

The former Barcelona academy graduate has also become a mainstay for his national side, featuring three times at the FIFA World Cup in the United States, Canada and Mexico, which landed Birmingham a cash windfall.

#2218. Birmingham City told to strike Nottingham Forest transfer for 95-cap international star

Birmingham City's impressive start to the season has got many fans at St. Andrew's @ Knighthead Park dreaming of a return to the Premier League for the first time since 2011.

Chris Davies' side have only lost two of their first 14 matches of the Championship season, winning four of their last five and scoring 14 goals in the process.

Tuesday's controlled 3-0 win over Millwall saw Birmingham reach the automatic promotion places for just over 24 hours, before they were knocked down to third for the time being after Wolves' win at Bristol City.

Such a strong start wasn't exactly forecast by many in this part of the Second City at the beginning of the season, as it was argued that the squad was left short in certain areas.

However, given the well-documented ambitions of American investors, Knighthead Capital Management, many are now debating what activity will be conducted in the winter window if Blues are still among the promotion contenders.

With Birmingham's forward department currently made up of August Priske, Jay Stansfield, Luis Vazquez and Marvin Ducksch, it has been questioned what changes will be made going forward.

Priske has already netted six times this season whilst Stansfield has now firmly aclimatised to playing as a 'number 10', whilst exit talk continues to surround Ducksch after a failed summer departure to Valencia, and Vazquez is yet to convince the majority of supporters.

As such, it was recently debated by Blues YouTuber, Tommy Kelsall, whether a reunion for Chris Wood, who scored 11 times in 29 appearances on loan in the 2011/12 season under Chris Hughton, makes sense, given his similarities with Priske, but also the experience under his belt.

The in-form striker was absent from Tuesday's dominant success over Millwall

Wood has only made two Premier League starts under Oliver Glasner at Nottingham Forest this season, having seen his 2025/26 campaign blighted by a knee injury.

Beforehand, the 95-time New Zealand international scored 20 times as the Reds secured European football in 2024/25, and his record of 170 strikes in the top two divisions is indisputable.

With his contract at the City Ground up next June, Football League World's Birmingham City fan pundit, Jason Moore, believes the 34-year-old would be a great option for Davies to call upon.

"I don't mind the shout," Moore told FLW.

"People might say he's aging, but he's, again, a proven Premier League striker. He's done a decent job in the Premier League.

"He's not pacey. But, I don't think that's his game and he'd probably be, like that tweet says, a like-for-like for August Priske in the way we play, so I'd be all up for it.

"You know he's going to work hard, he'd take that pressure off Priske playing every single game, and he's a miles better replacement than Luis Vazquez.

"If you can look at Chris Wood and if he's not playing at Forest, then I'd be all for it. If we could pull that off, that's a great shout. Him or Che (Adams), I'd be all up for it."

Blues fans, should Chris Davies attempt to sign Chris Wood in January? Comment below!

"I'd narrowly favour Che for his pace, but I'd be more than happy with Chris Wood if Che wasn't available. Both are excellent options."

Having a focal point in attack such as Priske has allowed Birmingham to source goals from across Davies' squad, and whilst the Dane has become a feared striker this term, Wood's experience would certainly aid a promotion bid.

Like Priske, he has the ability to bring fellow attackers into the game and make their own deep runs into the box and create chances, whilst also having a poacher's instinct despite not being particularly fast.

If Birmingham were also to end their Premier League exile, then a player of such calibre would also be worth retaining, given Wood's extensive record in both divisions.

The nature of the Championship schedule also means that the forward is likely to source more minutes in the West Midlands than he currently is in the East.

#2217. Derek McInnes makes Rangers transfer stance crystal clear over in-form star

Derek McInnes hopes Rangers can turn James Penrice’s loan move into a permanent deal after making his feelings clear to the Ibrox hierarchy.

The 27-year-old is currently on loan at Rangers from Greek side AEK Athens and has impressed since making the move to Glasgow.

Penrice’s form has now been rewarded with his first Scotland call-up, while McInnes has made no secret of his desire to keep the defender beyond his current agreement.

Asked whether discussions had taken place over making the move permanent, McInnes said: “No, I think, well, certainly I've made my feelings clear on it. Ideally, I would like to have done it as a permanent, but the best way forward at the time was a loan.

“But I think we're all aware of what it needs to be to make it permanent. But I think there's a lot of goodwill all round to try and that could be something that we'd like to do.

“The conversations I've had with Jim and the board have been along those lines, and hopefully we can make that permanent.”

Through AI, we'll rank the top 25 most intimidating football stadiums in British football, but which stadium comes out on top?

"I would prefer it if he stayed under the radar a little bit and stayed with Sheffield United. What I do think, though, is, now that there's no longer a need to sell at Bramall Lane, if Sydie Peck does get an offer that we find too good to refuse, then I think Wilder will be given a lot of the funds to strengthen the squad.

"We've been in this position before when we sold David Brooks, and we brought in players like John Egan and Ollie Norwood. But what is that price? I think it's at least £15 million. When you look at some of the prices that players are going for at this level now, he's an England U21 international who just scored a hat-trick in the Championship; at least £15 million. I'd be holding out for £20 million."

Do you agree with Jimmy's take on Sydie Peck? How much do you think Sheffield United should be commanding for the midfielder if interest were to arrive as early as January? Share your thoughts below!

Having been forced to part ways with Brooks, Hamer, and O'Hare in the summer, Sheffield United and their head coach Chris Wilder will have little intention of losing any other stars anytime soon.

Peck is certainly one of their prized assets right now, and under contract until the summer of 2028, are under no immediate pressure to cash in, although, a decision on his future must be made sooner rather than later.

The England youth international's ceiling is extraordinarily high, and Sheffield United will be hoping to be the continued beneficiaries of his services for years to come, as time will tell exactly how this saga plays out.

#2214. Unbeaten run, a rare free-kick and more: All the key stats from AC Milan 3-1 Inter

As is often the case with the derby win, there were a lot of fun facts and stats to delve into after the full-time whistle, including a free-kick rarity from Diego Moreira.

Many headed into the Derby della Madonnina with a lot of worry considering Inter’s strong start to the season, as well as Milan’s mid-week defeat to Bologna. However, it turned out to be a great evening for the Rossoneri, who clinched all three points in a 3-1 win.

Diego Moreira played a key role in the success, bagging one goal and two assists, while Ruben Amorim got his tactics right as well. In short, it was just what they needed and as is often the case after a huge win, a lot of fun stats emerged afterwards.

Starting with the general stats, it’s clear that Milan made the most of their chances with a 0.66 xG and three goals scored. Moreira’s free-kick obviously didn’t yield a high xG, nor did Adrien Rabiot’s tidy effort, and Christian Pulisic’s header wasn’t super easy to pull off.

Inter had some good chances from close range, parried by Mike Maignan, so that is probably why their xG is higher. Alessandro Bastoni’s header was also an easy one, unlike Pulisic’s, but it was nice to see Milan be the efficient team for once.

The possession is linked to the above, really, as Milan sat back a lot after scoring their goals. Tactically, they played it to perfection and Inter didn’t really create much with all that possession in the end.

This deserves a section of its own because Milan’s recent record in the derby is truly amazing. After losing six games on the trot, the Rossoneri have certainly turned things around with six wins in the last eight encounters and no defeats!

Now the Inter fans can feel some of the pain that we experienced a while back… It’s not just that the Nerazzurri as a club have been really poor against Milan, it’s also that Cristian Chivu has failed completely.

Winning trophies is the most important thing, he’s absolutely right, but there is no question that the derby record is bothering him. As reported by Opta, he’s only the third manager to lose his first three derbies after Leonardo (2009-2011) and Massimo Giacomini (1979-1980).

Like we said at the start, a lot of fun facts emerged from the win last night and giving each of them a section would be a little silly. From a rare free-kick goal in the derby to a midfielder in red-hot form, we have listed them below.

Moreira’s free-kick: Since Opta started collecting the stats in 2004-05, Milan have only scored three direct free-kick goals against Inter in all competitions, with Moreira’s being the latest one. Pirlo (2007) and Theo Hernandez (2025) were the first two.

Pulisic’s speedy start: Pulisic’s goal is the fastest Milan have scored in a league derby against Inter since the 2010-11 season, when Alexandre Pato found the back of the net after a mere 43 seconds. Talk about getting off the right foot.

Rabiot’s goal contributions: Rabiot has become the third Milan midfielder to have at least one goal contribution in five consecutive league games since 2004-05, following in the footsteps of Hakan Calhanoglu (in 2020) and Ricky Kaka (2004 and 2008).

#2213. Man Utd transfer news: United make breakthrough in January left-back pursuit but 'main issue' remains

By Lewis Blain | 04 Nov 2026 12:11

Manchester United have made progress in their shock pursuit of Manchester City defender Rayan Ait-Nouri, with the Algeria international reportedly open to leaving the Etihad Stadium.

The 25-year-old left-back is understood to be interested in a move to Old Trafford, while personal terms are not expected to present a problem.

However, the rivalry between the two Manchester clubs could prove a significant obstacle as United explore their January transfer options.

According to Sky Germany's Florian Plettenberg, Ait-Nouri is keen on leaving Manchester City during the January transfer window, with United still interested in his signature, having tried to sign him on deadline day.

Plettenberg reports that personal terms would not be an issue either, suggesting the Algeria international could be receptive to a switch across Manchester.

However, the journalist identifies the relationship between City and United as the 'main issue' that still remains, with Ait-Nouri's potential market currently being explored.

The former Wolverhampton Wanderers star has struggled to establish himself at City since arriving in 2025, making a departure increasingly plausible.

United's interest represents a potential opportunity to address their left-back situation, although negotiating directly with their local rivals would be a particularly complicated transfer.

There is little doubt that Ait-Nouri would offer United an attractive solution at left-back, but signing a City player would be a controversial move given the rivalry between the clubs.

United may therefore be better served exploring alternatives, particularly Barcelona's Alejandro Balde and RB Leipzig's David Raum.

Balde has struggled for regular opportunities under Hansi Flick, and at just 22, he would offer considerable long-term potential and could be tempted by the prospect of becoming United's first-choice left-back.

Raum is a different proposition, having established himself as a regular starter and captain at Leipzig. However, his contract expires in 2027, potentially allowing United to negotiate a deal.

Ait-Nouri remains an interesting option, especially with personal terms reportedly straightforward, but United should not allow the Manchester rivalry to dictate their recruitment.

Balde and Raum could offer more straightforward routes to strengthening the position, making it sensible for United to keep alternative targets firmly in their plans.

#2212. Scott Parker has been sent £18m West Ham message and Spurs are laughing loudest

Scott Parker’s start to life as West Ham head coach got off to a thrilling start.

The Hammers drew 3-3 against Cardiff City on Wednesday night in his first game in the dugout, with the team coming from 3-0 behind to secure a point on the road.

Goals from Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman sealed an exciting comeback in the second half, with the London outfit hoping that they can compete for automatic promotion this season.

But Parker’s first game as manager showed that there is perhaps more work to be done at the London Stadium than initially thought.

The first issue that Parker may have to address as the new West Ham boss is the underwhelming form of summer arrival Manor Solomon.

The winger was brought in with the expectation that he could power the team to promotion, much like he did at Leeds United in 2024/25, where he contributed 10 goals and 12 assists in 39 Championship appearances.

Should Scott Parker drop Manor Solomon? Comment your opinion below

But his form has been inconsistent at the London Stadium so far, and another underwhelming performance on Wednesday night led to him being hauled off after 69 minutes.

While he managed to complete 13 of his 15 attempted passes, the overall total matches that of someone quite uninvolved in the game, and he also failed to create a chance or take a clear shot at goal, leading to a deserved substitution (all stats from Fotmob).

Edvin Austbo was chosen to replace Solomon for the final 20 minutes against Cardiff, making his 10th appearance in the league this season.

This was the eighth time he came off the bench, and his lively performance helped the team in their comeback from 3-0 down.

West Ham United considered the potential appointment of former Tottenham Hotspur and Liverpool coach Johnny Heitinga before deciding on Scott Parker

The Norwegian already has as many goals and assists as Solomon so far this campaign, despite playing only 347 minutes compared to 1,003.

The 21-year-old arrived from Viking IF in a deal worth £18 million in the summer, and now could be the time for Parker to give him an extended run in the team ahead of Solomon.

Austbo hasn’t had an extended run in the team, which impacts how we can judge him, but his appearances so far have shown a lot of promise.

The winger has an expected assists (xA) per 90 of 0.53, creating 0.53 big chances per 90.

He also has 1.32 successful crosses each game at a success rate of 35.7 per cent, which makes him a dangerous threat in the final third, all while also taking 2.63 shots each game himself.

If he can replicate these kinds of performances over a series of starts in the coming weeks, then Parker will have no choice but to keep him in the lineup going forward over Solomon.

West Ham’s rivals Tottenham Hotspur signed Solomon in 2023, but he struggled for game time under various managers at the London outfit, which led to loan spells at Leeds, Villarreal and Fiorentina.

But the Hammers committed to a £5 million deal to sign the winger despite his lack of impact at Spurs.

Given the question marks over his quality now, the Premier League side have had the last laugh on their rival.

Parker dropping him to the bench would be the ultimate vindication for Spurs’ decision to cash in on the forward last summer.

Sunderland and Dinamo Zagreb continue their respective Europa League campaigns with a league phase fixture on Thursday night.

With both clubs experiencing disappointing seasons, this will be viewed as a key match by the hierarchies at both clubs.

Here, Sports Mole provides details of how to tune into the match at the home of the Premier League team.

The match between Sunderland and Dinamo Zagreb will kick off at 8pm, UK time.

This Europa League match will take place at the Stadium of Light, the home ground of Sunderland.

Boasting a capacity of 49,000, the stadium is no stranger to hosting major fixtures, with three senior England men's fixtures having being held at the Stadium of Light between 1999 and 2016.

Sunderland's showdown with the Croatian giants will be shown on TNT Sports 1 for viewers in the UK, with coverage also available on TNT Sports Ultimate.

Fans with access to TNT Sports can stream the match through HBO Max provided their subscription includes the relevant sports coverage.

Match highlights will be available through TNT Sports' digital platforms and YouTube channel, while both clubs are also expected to share footage and reaction through their official channels.

Dinamo Zagreb sit in second position in the league table, four points behind Hadjuk Split.

#2207. Rob Edwards backed to replace John Eustace at Derby County - but Middlesbrough 'concern' raised

It has been a nightmare start to the season for Derby County in the Championship.

After missing out on the play-offs on the final day of last season, many expected Derby to mount another promotion push this time around, but it has certainly not worked out that way.

It was another disastrous night for Derby as they were beaten 4-0 by Norwich City at Pride Park on Tuesday night, with goals from Anthony Musaba, Paris Maghoma, Darlin Yongwa and Jovon Makama sealing an emphatic victory for the Canaries.

The Rams' latest defeat extended their winless run to 10 games, and while they remain 23rd in the Championship table, they are now seven points from safety after winning just one of their opening 14 matches.

Despite increasing numbers of supporters calling for head coach John Eustace to be sacked, the 47-year-old will be in charge for the visit of Bolton Wanderers on Friday night, but with the November international break looming, he will be desperate for a result against the 17th-placed Trotters.

Should Derby part company with Eustace, one name that has been suggested as a potential replacement is Rob Edwards, who has been out of work since leaving Wolverhampton Wanderers in June following their relegation from the Premier League.

Edwards' most notable achievement came when he led Luton Town to promotion to the top flight via the play-offs in 2023, while he has also had spells with the likes of Forest Green Rovers, Watford and Middlesbrough, and he has been linked with a number of Championship clubs this season, including Southampton, Burnley and Wrexham.

When asked for his thoughts on the possible appointment of Edwards, FLW's Derby County fan pundit Shaun Woodward said that he would be happy to see the 43-year-old replace Eustace, but he admitted that he has concerns about his lack of loyalty after he walked away from Middlesbrough after just 16 games in charge to join Wolves last November.

"Forget Rob Edwards, I'd probably take Rob Earnshaw right now with the way things are going at Derby," Shaun told FLW.

"John Eustace has lost the dressing room, he sounds like a defeated man when he's doing his interviews post-match.

"We're shipping goals at an alarming rate, we're not looking like we're going to score, there's no fight in the team.

"They all look like they're out for themselves, they're hiding, it's a horrid, horrid time to be a Derby fan right now.

"We desperately need a change of manager.

"Rob Edwards has got experience of getting promotion out of this league at Luton, he did well in the Premier League as well with limited resources.

"My only problem with him is when he was at Middlesbrough and seemingly had a good thing going there, his head was turned, and he left to go to Wolves, which worries me.

"We've had quite a few managers over the years that have done well at Derby, and then a bigger club has come looking and they've left.

"That worries me with Rob Edwards, would he do the same again?

"Let's say he got us up the table this season, next season he gets us promoted or in and around it, and then another club comes looking and he leaves us.

"We want stability at the club, so that's my only concern with Edwards, but I would take him right now because something has to change.

While Edwards had a strong connection to Wolves, his decision to leave Middlesbrough while they were second in the Championship to take over at a club that looked doomed to relegation from the Premier League was questionable, and it will be a red flag in the minds of prospective employers.

With the club currently sitting seven points from safety, it seems Derby could be involved in a battle for survival this season, and it would be a risk for them to appoint a manager who has two relegations on his CV with Luton and Wolves, even if there are mitigating circumstances for those failures.

Would Rob Edwards be a good replacement for John Eustace at Derby County? Tell us in the comments below!

With the international break coming up after Friday's game against Bolton, the Rams should use the time to make a much-needed managerial change, but the jury is out over whether Edwards would be the right man to succeed Eustace.

#2206. Will Rodri face England? Barcelona and Spain star's return timeline revealed after huge injury blow

By Lewis Blain | 04 Nov 2026 12:38

Barcelona and Spain have suffered a significant injury setback after Rodri was ruled out for around a month with a hamstring problem.

The midfielder is set to miss Blaugrana's upcoming meeting with Atletico Madrid, as well as Spain's two Nations League fixtures against Czechia and England.

His absence could provide England with a major boost ahead of their trip to Spain, with Rodri remaining an influential figure for club and country.

According to journalist Fabrizio Romano, Rodri has suffered a hamstring injury that is expected to keep him out of action for approximately one month.

The 30-year-old sustained the problem during Barcelona's 3-0 victory over Aston Villa in the Champions League on Tuesday night, requesting a substitution late in the first half after experiencing discomfort in his right hamstring.

Hansi Flick withdrew the midfielder at half-time to prevent further damage, introducing academy graduate Marc Bernal alongside Pedri.

Bernal delivered a composed performance as Barca maintained their defensive shape and secured their first Champions League clean sheet in 18 matches.

However, Rodri's injury means he will miss this weekend's meeting with Atletico, while his recovery timeline also rules him out of Spain's upcoming UEFA Nations League commitments.

Luis de la Fuente's side are scheduled to travel to Czechia before hosting England, with the midfielder's absence depriving Spain of one of their most important players.

?⚠️ Rodri suffers hamstring injury and will be out of action for Barcelona and Spain for the next month. Out for Barça next game against Atlético this weekend. ❌ https://t.co/owY6co3e0A

Losing Rodri for a month is a significant setback for both Barcelona and Spain, given his ability to dictate the tempo, protect the defence and provide composure in possession.

His importance to Barcelona this season is reflected in his statistics, having featured in 14 matches across all competitions, accumulating 946 minutes and an average match rating of 7.28.

That makes him the club's fifth most-used player this season, underlining how regularly he has featured in Flick's plans.

For Spain, his absence against England is arguably even more concerning.

Rodri has consistently been one of the most influential midfielders in international football, and replacing his positional intelligence and ability to control matches is no straightforward task.

The Three Lions could therefore receive a considerable boost, potentially giving Thomas Tuchel's side greater opportunities to disrupt Spain's usual possession-based approach.

Spain still possess considerable midfield quality, but Rodri's absence could change the complexion of the contest, particularly against an England side looking to exploit any weaknesses in the hosts' midfield.

#2204. Martin O'Neill opens up on Celtic fan meeting and denies ulterior motive

Martin O’Neill has opened up on his decision to face Celtic supporters at a recent meeting in Greenock and insists the questions fired at the club were “fair”.

The Hoops manager attended the gathering to hear first-hand the concerns of fans amid growing discontent over the running of the club and recent results.

O’Neill revealed he only became aware of the meeting at the “11th hour”, but was determined to attend and get a better understanding of the mood among supporters.

“I just wanted to go down to it and essentially experience what the supporters are feeling and see how we can do something about it,” he said.

Asked about the main grievances raised during the meeting, O’Neill suggested there was little unexpected about the issues put to those representing Celtic.

“I think it's straightforward,” he said. “I think you would know what they would be.

“I think they were fair, absolutely. And I think that the board as a whole tried to answer them as honestly as they possibly could.”

Real Madrid welcomes sporting royalty as tennis icon joins board of directors Scotland on alert as Slovenia rocked by fan mutiny before crunch Nations League clash

O’Neill also insisted the strength of feeling expressed by supporters did not come as a surprise.

“No, I don't think anything really surprised me at all,” he said.

“I think that the questions that they were asking, I think that the board would have been expecting those questions to be asked and obviously hoping to answer them in the best possible manner, or as honestly as they could.”

The Celtic boss revealed it was not the first time he had attended such an event, having gone along to a similar gathering several months ago, and stressed he would be prepared to meet supporters again if his schedule permits.

However, O’Neill dismissed the suggestion that attending the Greenock meeting alongside club representatives could be viewed as an attempt to shore up his own position.

“My job doesn't depend on going to meetings like that,” he said. “My job depends on results. It really does, and I cannot forget that.

“If I am the manager and the next meeting comes up and time allows, I don't mind going to it.”

#2202. Arsenal transfer news: Mikel Arteta 'ponders big move' for versatile £70m attacker in January window

By Lewis Blain | 04 Nov 2026 13:11

Arsenal are continue to keep a close eye on Atletico Madrid forward Alex Baena as manager Mikel Arteta considers strengthening his attacking options ahead of the January transfer window.

The 25-year-old Spain international has enjoyed an impressive start to the campaign, attracting interest from several of Europe's leading clubs.

However, Atletico's reluctance to sell and a potential £70m asking price could complicate the Gunners' hopes of securing his signature this winter.

The Premier League champions retain a long-standing interest in Baena and Arteta is 'pondering a big move' for him in 2027, as per TEAMtalk.

Arsenal tracked the Spanish attacker during his time at Villarreal and have continued to monitor his progress since his £40m move to Atletico in summer 2025.

Baena has registered five goals and four assists in 14 appearances this season, taking his career total to 92 goal contributions in 253 matches.

His versatility is a major attraction, with the 25-year-old capable of operating on the left, centrally or even as a makeshift number nine.

Arsenal view him as an option to strengthen their attacking depth, particularly after allowing Gabriel Martinelli and Leandro Trossard to leave during the summer.

However, Atletico are determined to retain Baena, who is contracted until 2030, and would only consider an exceptional January offer. That has led to the belief that a move would be more realistic next summer, with a fee of at least £70m required to tempt the Spanish club into negotiations.

Bayern Munich are also seriously interested, while top-flight rivals Tottenham Hotspur, Manchester City, Newcastle United and Aston Villa have monitored his situation.

Baena's growing reputation is hardly surprising given his performances for both club and country.

His nine goal contributions in 14 appearances already this season demonstrate his increasing influence, while his involvement in Spain's 2026 World Cup triumph over the summer has further enhanced his profile.

For Arsenal, his ability to operate across multiple attacking positions would be particularly valuable. Arteta needs another high-quality option on the left, especially with Eberechi Eze yet to consistently impress when deployed there and recent injuries to alternatives like Christos Tzolis.

Baena would offer creativity, goals and tactical flexibility, making him an attractive addition to an already talented forward line. However, Atletico have little reason to sell midway through the season, meaning Arsenal may need to wait until the summer and prepare a substantial offer.

At £70m, it would be a major investment, but Baena's versatility and proven quality at the highest level make him an intriguing target for Arteta.

The ‘worst’ of the starters was Goncalo Ramos at 6.5, while Sankhoun Diawara wasn’t too far ahead at 6.8. In total, 325 fans submitted their player ratings via our tool, so the ratings were certainly based on the views of a lot of Milan fans.

#2200. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2198. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2196. Melvine Malard becomes Chelsea’s leading scorer as FOUR-goal start strengthens her claim to the No.9 role

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Melvine Malard’s Chelsea goals are beginning to provide a persuasive answer to the club’s centre-forward question.

Her opener in Sunday’s 3-0 victory over West Ham took the summer signing to four competitive goals, making her Chelsea’s leading scorer this season.

That total comprises two goals in Champions League qualifying and two in the Women’s Super League.

The circumstances make those contributions particularly useful. Across three scoring appearances, Malard has broken a deadlock, restored a lead and helped Chelsea put difficult matches beyond their opponents.

Three of those goals arrived while Chelsea were level or protecting a one-goal advantage.

That gives the early return significance beyond its size. Malard has repeatedly contributed while there was still a contest to resolve.

Sunday provided the latest example. Chelsea had plenty of possession but needed somebody to turn an opening into a lead.

Turn and finish 💥Melvine Malard finishes for @ChelseaFCW 🔵#BarclaysWSL pic.twitter.com/eicBNrYMYk

According to Chelsea’s official match report, the Blues reached the half-hour mark without a shot on target despite having 64 per cent possession.

Sjoeke Nüsken created the breakthrough by winning possession and finding Malard.

The striker received the ball with her back to goal, held her position, turned and finished into the bottom corner.

It was an important demonstration of what she can offer centrally. Chelsea could find her under pressure and still get a shot away, rather than needing to release her into open space.

Nüsken’s contribution deserves equal recognition. Her pressing created the opportunity that Malard converted.

The sequence offers Bompastor something useful to build on: winning possession higher up the pitch and finding a forward capable of finishing before the opposition recover.

Malard’s first three Chelsea goals came as a substitute.

Her two-goal competitive debut against Real Sociedad immediately demonstrated that capacity to change a match.

Nüsken released her for the goal that restored Chelsea’s lead at 3-2. Ellie Carpenter subsequently supplied her second, extending the advantage to three goals.

The official account of that European victory records two different routes to goal: a run beyond the defence and a finish from a delivery across the box.

Against Birmingham, her alertness produced another valuable contribution. Malard punished hesitation over a backpass to score Chelsea’s second, eight minutes after coming on, as detailed in the club’s match report.

Those appearances give Bompastor flexibility. Malard has already influenced matches when introduced against tiring opponents, while West Ham supplied a scoring return from the starting role.

Chelsea signed Malard from Manchester United on a four-year contract, bringing her back together with a coach who first identified her potential at 14.

Bompastor later coached her in Lyon’s senior side. Their relationship therefore predates this summer’s recruitment by years.

Speaking when Chelsea announced her signing, Malard said:

“My objective is to become better every single day and to win trophies.”

Her next task is sustaining this contribution across a longer run of matches.

Competition remains productive. Aggie Beever-Jones replaced her at West Ham and supplied Maika Hamano’s late goal, giving Bompastor another reason to value both forwards.

Malard’s strongest argument is the range of situations she has already influenced. She has attacked space, finished crosses, punished hesitation and scored with a defender close behind her.

Four goals offer an encouraging early return. The variety behind them gives Chelsea something substantial to develop.

#2195. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2194. Chelsea: Morgan Rogers names area to improve despite superb start

By Axel Clody | 04 Nov 2026 13:42

Morgan Rogers did not need long to justify Chelsea’s investment. With six goals and four assists in 12 matches, he has become one of Chelsea’s key players this season. He is directly involved in the build-up play and is gradually establishing himself among the players who excite the fans.

This immediate impact does not mean the Englishman considers his work finished, however. On the contrary, the player himself acknowledges that he still has room to improve in one of the most important aspects for someone with his profile: the ability to turn chances into goals.

Rogers’ start becomes even more significant when the context of his arrival is considered. Chelsea spent about £117m to sign him from Aston Villa, placing considerable expectation on his shoulders before he had even pulled on the blue shirt.

In a transfer of that size, there is little room for a quiet adaptation period or a season of getting used to the environment. The pressure for an immediate impact has followed him since day one, and he is coping with it on the pitch.

? Fermin Lopez, Morgan Rogers and Nico Paz: Club stats this season ?? Fermin Lopez: 6 goals, 6 assists ??????? Morgan Rogers: 6 goals, 4 assists ?? Nico Paz: 5 goals, 6 assists All making their mark! ? https://t.co/o1GsWqphG7

Rogers’ attacking contribution helps explain why he has already won over part of the stands. His numbers show a player who not only finds the net but can also create for his team-mates and influence the development of moves.

The combination of goals and assists shows how much influence he has in attack, while his ability to carry the ball and create danger makes him an important part of how the team functions.

There is nevertheless a difference between producing flashes of brilliance and delivering a complete performance over 90 minutes. That is precisely where the Englishman’s game can step up.

Rogers has shown he can change situations, but the next step is to sustain that influence more consistently, shaping matches without relying solely on isolated moments.

The player does not hide where he believes he needs to improve. In a recent interview, Rogers admitted that his efficiency in front of goal and his ability to convert the simplest chances are aspects that still deserve attention in his development.

‘I think being more clinical in front of goal, being more ruthless in and around the box, is something that previously I was a bit disappointed in myself because I’d score a nice goal every once in a while, but I wouldn’t score the easy goals.’

‘I’m trying to work on that here. I’m trying to improve in my final actions, so that’s my biggest one. I’m an attacker, so I try to score goals and assist and create chances. I want to keep improving. I’m not there yet but it’s a good start,’ he concluded.

The assessment of Morgan Rogers also comes down to consistency. The Englishman has already shown that he can be decisive at different moments, but his attacking contribution can still translate into more complete performances.

The difference lies in being able to influence the game continuously, without his impact being concentrated in a few standout moments.

This room for improvement does not diminish the merit of what he is achieving. On the contrary, it shows the potential of a player who, despite carrying the weight of one of the most expensive signings in football history, has started the season with numbers and a leading role.

Rogers was expected to take on responsibility when he arrived at Chelsea, and his first months suggest he is living up to it.

The next stage, however, demands more than maintaining the current output. If he can improve his decision-making in the final moments and make better use of his chances in the box, Rogers could stop being only a player capable of producing great moments and become a dominant presence throughout matches.

#2193. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

#2192. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2191. Keira Walsh adds another Chelsea weapon as former Arsenal target scores THIRD goal of the season

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Keira Walsh is giving Chelsea opponents another decision to make: protect the penalty area or step out to stop her shooting.

Her goal in Sunday’s 3-0 victory at West Ham took her to three for the season across all competitions. Each has come from outside the box.

For a player whose passing helps organise Chelsea’s attacks, that threat adds another way to influence matches. Recent performances show her contributing both to the construction of chances and their finish.

Against Sociedad, Walsh struck from distance after a corner, helping Chelsea complete their passage into the Champions League’s league phase.

The club’s account of that qualifying victory describes a tight encounter in which clear opportunities were limited.

At Manchester United, Lucy Bronze found her near the edge of the box and Walsh drove her finish into the bottom corner.

She also supplied Lauren James’ goal that afternoon, as Chelsea’s match report records. It was a useful illustration of the choices she offers: attempt the finish herself or release somebody closer to goal.

West Ham provided another example of that balance.

Walsh had already struck the post before Alyssa Thompson carried the ball in from the left and found Wieke Kaptein. Kaptein moved it on to Walsh, whose finish gave Chelsea breathing space.

The official report also records an earlier Walsh run down the channel and delivery towards Sjoeke Nüsken.

Her involvement therefore extended beyond waiting for opportunities to shoot. She helped Chelsea approach the penalty area from different positions.

There was a similar reward against Arsenal a week earlier. Walsh’s saved effort fell to Thompson for the only goal, as covered in our report of Chelsea’s derby victory.

That sequence illustrates why a dangerous shot can matter even when the goalkeeper stops it. Chelsea had a runner ready to attack the rebound.

Our assessment is that Walsh’s shooting gives defenders an additional problem. Closing her down can open a passing route; allowing her time leaves the goalkeeper exposed to a player already finding her range.

Walsh has also explained why she feels comfortable alongside Potter.

Speaking after the Arsenal victory in an interview published by Chelsea, she said:

“Lexi is really intelligent. She makes me feel really comfortable when I’m playing next to her.”

Her wider assessment emphasised collective responsibility, with team-mates covering for each other and working to make Chelsea harder to beat.

That matters when assessing midfield influence. Walsh’s freedom to help an attack depends partly on the positioning and decisions of those around her.

Potter subsequently started alongside her in Chelsea’s confirmed team against Lyon, giving their partnership another demanding European assignment.

The encouraging feature is the trust Walsh describes. Bompastor has an experienced organiser working with a younger midfielder whose reading of the game she clearly values.

BBC Sport reported that Barcelona rejected Arsenal’s bid for Walsh in September 2024.

Chelsea completed her signing in January 2025 on a four-and-a-half-year contract. Her official club profile outlines the technical quality and ability to dictate tempo that underpinned that recruitment.

Those qualities remain central to her value. Three early goals strengthen the attacking contribution around them, without establishing that she has changed position or should abandon her deeper responsibilities.

For Bompastor, the opportunity is to preserve that balance: Walsh helping Chelsea progress through midfield, supporting those around her and recognising when the space opens for a finish.

#2189. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2188. CAF African Player of the Year: 10 nominees revealed with Yan Diomande missing, three names spark debate

By Axel Clody | 04 Nov 2026 13:52

The Confederation of African Football (CAF) on Wednesday revealed its list of 10 nominees for the 2026 African Ballon d’Or. It is a prestigious selection, but several choices raise eyebrows.

Three Moroccans, three Senegalese and no sign of Ibrahim Maza or Yan Diomande: the list of 10 candidates for the 2026 African Player of the Year award will fuel plenty of discussion.

Achraf Hakimi, Brahim Diaz, Ismael Saibari, Sadio Mane, Ismaila Sarr, Pape Gueye, Amad Diallo, Yoane Wissa, Antoine Semenyo and Teboho Mokoena have been selected by the Confederation of African Football.

?????? ?? ??? ???? (???) - ???????? #CAFAwards2026 https://t.co/jgE5qPNl4C

The reigning champion, Achraf Hakimi, was crowned in 2025 ahead of Mohamed Salah and Victor Osimhen. The Morocco captain has strong arguments to win a second consecutive award, given his performances with Paris Saint-Germain and Morocco’s run at the World Cup.

He would become the first player to retain the award since Mohamed Salah in 2018 and the first Moroccan to win it twice. Ismael Saibari, who has had a particularly accomplished year, is the other Moroccan candidate, while Brahim Diaz completes Morocco’s strong representation.

The three Senegalese players could turn into a handicap for their country. Sadio Mane remains the most prestigious name, while Ismaila Sarr, brilliant in the Conference League with Crystal Palace, and Pape Gueye have played a part in Senegal’s excellent year. They risk splitting the votes, whereas Hakimi appears the most obvious Moroccan candidate.

The absences of Ibrahim Maza and Yan Diomande are surprising, particularly given some of the names selected. Both are among the candidates for the African Young Player of the Year award.

Maza has established himself at Bayer Leverkusen and with Algeria, while Diomande, who became the most expensive African player in history in the summer transfer window, has stepped up a level at RB Leipzig, Real Madrid and with the Ivory Coast national team.

Their youth does nothing to diminish the value of their performances: age should be neither an advantage nor a ceiling. They finished ninth and fifth respectively in the 2026 Kopa Trophy.

The inclusion of Amad Diallo, despite his qualities and growing importance at Manchester United, appears questionable at the expense of Diomande.

The selections of Yoane Wissa and especially Teboho Mokoena can also be debated. The South African from Mamelodi Sundowns remains a reference on the continent, but his year appears less striking individually than those of several players left out.

Cape Verde goalkeeper Vozinha could also have laid claim to a place after accompanying his country’s historic run. The CAF selected only outfield players, leaving Cape Verde without a representative despite an exceptional year. Even before the list is reduced to three finalists and the CAF Awards ceremony on 7 December in Lagos, Nigeria, the first selection has already produced some major losers.
