#2186. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

#2183. Getafe vs. Brighton: How to watch, date, time, live stream and TV channel for Conference League fixture

By Matthew Cooper | 04 Nov 2026 13:58

Getafe and Brighton meet in the UEFA Conference League on November 5, with the Spanish side welcoming the Seagulls to the Coliseum for matchday three of the league phase.

The hosts currently sit 20th in the table and are searching for their first win, while the visitors are unbeaten in ninth.

Here, Sports Mole details everything you need to know about how to follow Thursday's match.

The match will kick off at 8pm UK time on Thursday, November 5.

The fixture will take place at the Coliseum, which is the home stadium of Getafe.

The ground has a capacity of 16,500 and Getafe have played there since 1998.

The Conference League match will be shown live on TNT Sports 2, with coverage beginning at 7pm UK time.

UK viewers can stream the match live via HBO Max after TNT Sports moved from Discovery+ to HBO Max earlier this year.

Viewers can access the stream through a standalone TNT Sports subscription or by adding TNT Sports to selected HBO Max entertainment packages.

Highlights of the match will be available on UEFA's website, HBO Max and the TNT Sports Football YouTube channel, while TNT Sports will also post clips on their social media channels.

Getafe have failed to pick up a win in Europe so far this season, drawing 1-1 with Romanian side Universitatea Craiova and losing 5-2 to Swiss team Lugano in their opening fixtures.

Jose Bordalas's men need a positive result against Brighton to boost their hopes of reaching the knockouts and they will take heart from the fact they beat Sevilla 3-1 in La Liga on Sunday.

Brighton, meanwhile, are competing in Europe for the first time since 2024 and they have made a strong start.

The Seagulls have four points from their first two league phase games, having thumped Lithuanian club Kauno Zalgiris 4-0 before drawing 0-0 with Czech side Jablonec.

#2182. Breaking: Man Utd begin formal progress for Michael Carrick replacement

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United are reportedly looking at options beyond Michael Carrick after the club believes he is not the right option for United.

Exclusive 💣Manchester United begin process to identify next head coach, sources indicate.Club sources have confirmed that Manchester United have started formal progress on appointing a new manager.Representatives of Julian Nagelsmann are open to listening to the club’s…

United have not announced any change in their managerial plans, so the report remains unconfirmed. Carrick is still in charge, but his results at the start of the new season have given the club plenty to think about.

#2181. Celtic treble winner formally announces bid to become FIFA president

Former Celtic defender Ramon Vega has formally announced his candidacy to become the next president of FIFA.

The ex-Switzerland international confirmed that he intends to stand in the FIFA presidential election scheduled for March 18, 2027 in Rabat, Morocco.

Vega, who enjoyed a memorable spell at Celtic during the 2000/01 Treble-winning campaign under Martin O'Neill, launched his bid with a strongly worded statement calling for major changes to the way world football is governed.

The 55-year-old declared that "football belongs to the people" as he outlined his desire to restore trust in the sport's governing body.

Vega said: "After more than a decade of preparation, I formally announce my candidacy for the Presidency of FIFA in 2027.

"I bring the mentality of a player, the discipline of a defender and decades of experience from the pitch to the boardroom.

How Celtic trio fared in B Team defeat to Ross County as O'Neill watches on Joel van den Berg recovery latest as three Celtic players set for B Team duty

"I defended my team on the field. Now I am ready to defend football itself, its integrity, its people and its future."

Vega also insisted his campaign was not motivated by "personal ambition, power or privilege", describing his candidacy as a push for reconciliation and fundamental change within world football.

He added: "No individual is greater than football. No institution should ever place itself above the game.

"This is not merely an announcement of candidacy. It is a call for fundamental change, a commitment to reconciliation and a vision for a new era of world football."

📣 OFFICIAL ANNOUNCEMENT I FORMALLY ANNOUNCE MY CANDIDACY FOR THE FIFA PRESIDENTIAL ELECTION — 18 MARCH 2027 RABAT, MOROCCO FOOTBALL BELONGS TO THE PEOPLE — NOT TO THOSE WHO CONTROL IT. Football stands at a defining crossroads. The decisions we make today will shape the future of our beautiful game for generations to come. WE DECLARE ZERO TOLERANCE for leadership that has humiliated the passion of five billion football fans around the world and insulted the dignity, dedication and integrity of the millions of people who devote their lives to our beautiful game. Football belongs to its people. Their passion deserves respect, their voices deserve to be heard, and their contributions deserve recognition — never humiliation, intimidation or contempt. After more than a decade of preparation, I formally announce my candidacy for the Presidency of FIFA in 2027. Alongside me stands my Hungarian wife and First Lady, Claudia — a pioneer with over 25 years of experience in professional men s football. Together, we share an unwavering commitment to restoring trust, reforming governance and reuniting the global football family. I bring the mentality of a player, the discipline of a defender and decades of experience from the pitch to the boardroom. I defended my team on the field. Now I am ready to defend football itself — its integrity, its people and its future. My candidacy is not driven by personal ambition, power or privilege. It is driven by responsibility, conviction and a profound love for the game that has given me everything. I AM NOT STANDING TO CONTINUE THE WAR. I AM STANDING TO END IT. No individual is greater than football. No institution should ever place itself above the game. This is not merely an announcement of candidacy. It is a call for fundamental change, a commitment to reconciliation and a vision for a new era of world football. #2180. Chelsea’s £117m Morgan Rogers faced Bournemouth role problem as former scout explains why loan failed

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Morgan Rogers struggled to establish himself at Bournemouth because the role envisaged for him did not fit the team’s usual setup, according to former Cherries scout Des Taylor.

Chelsea’s £117million signing faces his former club on Saturday, with Taylor offering fresh insight into a difficult period in the attacker’s development.

Speaking to BBC Sport’s Nizaar Kinsella in a report syndicated by AOL, Taylor explained:

“We saw him as a number 10, but Parker was not really playing with a number 10.”

Rogers started only three matches and scored once during the loan under Scott Parker.

Taylor highlighted the combination of limited opportunities and appearances outside Rogers’ preferred position, while stressing that he did not blame Parker for the move failing to work.

It adds a specific explanation to the broader setbacks Rogers discussed in our earlier coverage of the experiences that shaped his Chelsea move.

Rogers approaches the reunion after scoring England’s seventh goal against Croatia, finishing after rounding the goalkeeper.

England face Czechia on Tuesday before Chelsea resume their Premier League campaign.

The Blues’ official weekly schedule confirms Bournemouth’s visit for Saturday at 3pm, giving Rogers the opportunity to face the club where his early progress stalled.

#2179. Man Utd told £50m offer could tempt Fulham to sell highly rated teenager

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United could face an expensive battle to sign Fulham youngster Josh King, with offers exceeding £50million potentially enough to persuade the London club to consider a sale.

The 19-year-old has attracted attention with his performances this season, despite Fulham’s difficult results.

Manchester United are among several clubs monitoring his progress, alongside Liverpool, Arsenal, Borussia Dortmund and Barcelona.

Speaking on the Transfer Insider podcast, Pete O’Rourke explained that King’s growing reputation has put him on the radar of several major clubs.

“Josh King will be on the radar for a number of clubs because of his performances for Fulham so far this season,” O’Rourke said.

“It’s not just Liverpool, there’s been talk of the likes of Manchester United, Arsenal, Borussia Dortmund and even Barcelona looking at Josh King.”

Fulham are not actively planning to let the teenager leave in the near future as he is under contract until 2029. The contract length gives Fulham an upper hand, while interest from multiple clubs indicates a concession is not expected.

“Fulham are not planning for any early departure for Josh King. He is still under contract until 2029, and obviously they’ll want to keep him.”

The reporter also pointed to Fulham’s league position as a factor that could influence their decision. If they become embroiled in a relegation battle, losing one of their best performers would be a major risk.

“If Manchester United or any other club wanted to make an offer, he’s going to be worth in excess of £50million probably right now.”

#2178. Chelsea teenager Reggie Watson captains England and scores as Germany snatch victory with late comeback

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Chelsea midfielder Reggie Watson scored while captaining England Under-17s on Sunday, although Germany’s late comeback ended his international window with a 3-2 defeat.

The 16-year-old equalised from the penalty spot after Oscar Johnson drew a foul inside the area.

According to England’s official match report, Watson beat goalkeeper Nick Hartung with a finish into the left-hand corner.

Mylo Bernard put England ahead on the hour, but Linus Güther equalised seven minutes later. Niko Ilicevic then struck the winner in the 83rd minute.

Barry Lewtas’ side finished the three-match tournament with two victories and one defeat, having previously beaten Israel and Venezuela.

Watson’s involvement offers Chelsea supporters another opportunity to track a teenager already introduced to senior football by Xabi Alonso.

As covered in our report on Watson’s first-team progress, he started the Carabao Cup victory over Leeds United and played the opening 45 minutes.

That appearance, aged 16 years and 226 days, placed him fourth among Chelsea’s youngest competitive debutants.

His England captaincy and goal add a concrete international development to that emerging club story.

#2176. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

#2175. Bristol Rovers vs Cambridge United - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 04 Nov 2026 14:45, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 14:53

The oldest football competition in the world gets underway later this week for clubs across the English Football League, with League Two side Bristol Rovers welcoming third-tier Cambridge United to the Memorial Stadium in the FA Cup first round.

The Gas are currently enduring a seven-game winless run in all competitions and face a U's side managed by Neil Harris, who saw his team thrash Brighton & Hove Albion Under-21s 6-0 in the EFL Trophy earlier this week.

Hoping to reach the second round of the FA Cup for the second consecutive campaign, Bristol Rovers head into Friday night's encounter without a victory since beating Exeter City 1-0 in September.

Steve Evans, who is approaching one year in charge of Bristol Rovers, guided the Gas to a mid-table finish last season after overseeing a nine-game unbeaten run to end the campaign, although they were unable to mount a serious challenge for the playoff places.

Their encouraging form towards the end of last season initially carried into the 2026-27 campaign, with Rovers sitting third in League Two at the end of September after winning five of their opening eight matches.

However, since closing out that month with a narrow victory over the Grecians, Evans's side have endured a miserable October, suffering defeats to Barnet, Shrewsbury Town, Salford City and Fleetwood Town.

That poor run has seen the Gas fall out of the automatic promotion places and slide to 17th in the table, with their 16 points leaving them just four clear of the relegation zone.

Returning to the Memorial Stadium for the first time since their goalless draw with Swindon Town, Rovers will hope some FA Cup magic can help end their three-game winless run at home and rediscover the form that saw them set a club-record 11 consecutive victories on their own turf.

As for Cambridge United, they enter the FA Cup as a League One side for the first time since 2024, having secured an immediate return to the third tier last season by finishing third in League Two with 82 points.

Under former Millwall and Gillingham manager Harris, the U's have adapted well to life back in League One and currently sit just one point outside the playoff places, having collected 21 points from five wins, six draws and three defeats.

Despite their encouraging start, Cambridge are without a league victory in four matches, with draws against Wycombe Wanderers and Sheffield Wednesday coming alongside defeats to Plymouth Argyle and Leyton Orient.

However, following their loss at Brisbane Road to close out October, the U's returned to winning ways in emphatic fashion earlier this week, securing progression from Southern Group G in the EFL Trophy with a 6-0 demolition of Brighton & Hove Albion Under-21s at Cledara Abbey Stadium where Callum Stead scored twice in the convincing victory, while the visitors also had a player sent off.

With no league fixture for more than two weeks, Harris will be keen for his side to build on that momentum by reaching the second round of the FA Cup before turning their attention back to League One, where Cambridge will also be looking to cut down on the number of draws that have hindered their progress, having shared the points more often than any other side in the division.

The sides last met in the FA Cup in December 1971, when the Pirates claimed a 3-0 victory over Cambridge in the second round.

Cambridge United form (all competitions):

Having faced a Fleetwood Town side who produced a shock victory over Arsenal in the EFL Cup just days earlier, Bristol Rovers could name an unchanged starting XI, provided there are no fresh injury concerns.

Captain Alfie Kilgour is expected to remain at the heart of a back three, flanked by Keenan Gough and Irish defender Ciaran Kelly.

Club top scorer Fabrizio Cavegn should lead the line, while Jack Sparkes is likely to retain his place in midfield.

Cambridge, meanwhile, are expected to make several changes to the side that featured in the EFL Trophy during the week, although Harris could still hand opportunities to fringe players against opposition from the division below.

Jake Eastwood has been the U's first-choice goalkeeper this season but could make way for 22-year-old Gabriel Breeze, who kept a clean sheet against Brighton's youth side on Tuesday night.

Ben Knight was absent from the squad for that EFL Trophy victory but could return to the attack, with former Barnet forward Stead expected to lead the line once again.

Young; Gough, Kilgour, Kelly; James, Smallwood, Mola, Sparkes; Forde, Cavegn, Appiah

Cambridge United possible starting lineup:

Breeze; Heffernan, Watts, Perry; Bennett, Hoddle, McLoughlin, Bradshaw; Knight, Stead, Heath

Although neither side enjoyed a particularly impressive October, Cambridge will take confidence from their emphatic EFL Trophy victory earlier this week.

We expect the difference in league positions to prove decisive on Friday night, with the visitors securing a comfortable win to progress to the second round of the FA Cup.

#2174. Fabrizio Romano shares latest on Martinez contract after explosive rant

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Argentinian defender has also apologized to the United higher ups as well over his comments, as shared by Fabrizio Romano.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Martinez had questioned whether Carrick was backed enough in the summer and suggested the club needed changes.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

Speaking about the situation on his YouTube channel, as quoted by Football 365, Romano explained why Martinez’s comments caused concern inside the club.

“He spoke very strongly after the game against Atletico Madrid, which Man Utd lost. It was a very negative moment for the Red Devils. We know that Licha was very strong in his comments. He said that Michael Carrick was not backed enough during the summer transfer window.”

Romano added that Martinez had spoken directly to those at the club after the incident.

“Licha apologised to Man Utd as a club. He spoke to the directors, he spoke to Jason Wilcox, and he spoke to Michael Carrick, who was also involved in this conversation.”

“So, the underlying message remains the same. Lisandro is apologising for the way he expressed himself after a game during a difficult moment for Man Utd, but there was a discussion about the substance of what he said.”

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

“According to the information available tonight, Lisandro Martinez has been fined.”

He added that Martinez is expected to return to the squad as normal, with the fine considered sufficient punishment for the incident.

“The internal discussion also helped to resolve all the issues. For Man Utd, the case is closed.”

That should bring some immediate clarity to the situation, although questions remain about how the episode could affect Martinez’s longer-term position at Old Trafford.

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Romeo Lavia produced a superb defence-splitting assist for Belgium against France on Monday, although a late collapse left the Chelsea midfielder facing a 4-1 defeat.

Lavia found Dodi Lukebakio with a forward pass from inside his own half, sending the attacker through to score in the 35th minute.

#2172. Venezia vs Udinese - prediction, team news, lineups

By Jonathan O'Shea | 04 Nov 2026 15:04, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 15:06

The Serie A weekend will kick off with a battle at the bottom under Friday night lights, as Udinese visit Venezia at Stadio Penzo.

After Kosta Runjaic was sacked earlier this week, both clubs have already changed manager due to a truly dismal set of results.

Promoted back to Italy’s top flight last spring, Venezia have only picked up three points since returning to Serie A, and they are rooted to the foot of the table.

After a slow start, the Lagunari swapped Giovanni Stroppa for survival specialist Davide Nicola, whose new side impressively upset Napoli in his first match.

However, they have since lost their next three games without scoring; last weekend, they were beaten 1-0 by Como, despite the best efforts of busy goalkeeper Filip Stankovic.

As a result, Venezia are only the third team ever to lose at least nine of their first 10 Serie A fixtures, leaving Nicola with issues to fix at both ends.

Not only have the Arancioneroverdi scored just six league goals, they are currently conceding at a rate of two per game - undoubtedly relegation form.

Now, they will host opponents in similarly dire shape; as Udinese are just two points above them, victory would lift Venezia off the bottom rung.

Most date back several years, but Udinese have won five of the sides' last seven league meetings, and they came out on top in a Coppa Italia tie earlier this term.

However, they have only ever posted one top-flight victory at Stadio Penzo, and the Fruilani are currently in freefall.

After a 5-2 crushing by Roma last week, Udinese have lost seven of their last eight matches, and that heavy home defeat proved the final stand for Runjaic.

The German coach had fared well in his first two years at the club, but a tally of five points from 10 Serie A games represents their worst start this century.

Without missing a beat, ex-Venezia boss Paolo Zanetti was appointed until the end of the season, and he must first visit his former club.

Since getting the Lagunari promoted for the first time in 19 years, Zanetti's personal record has been mixed elsewhere, so he will surely have a point to prove on Friday.

Marin Sverko, Bartol Franjic and Andrea Adorante are still ruled out for Venezia, while wing-back Thierry Correia is struggling to recover from a thigh strain.

After Lion Lauerbach started the Lagunari's last two matches, it remains to be seen if John Yeboah reforms his partnership with club-record signing Akor Adams.

Only Roma star Donyell Malen (15) has had more shots on target than Adams (13) in Serie A this term, but the Nigerian striker has only scored twice - both times on home turf.

Meanwhile, Udinese's main man is enduring a much leaner run: since the end of March, Keinan Davis has gone 13 league games without finding the net.

Attacking midfielder Jurgen Ekkelenkamp is the Friulani's top Serie A scorer with three goals, and he should certainly feature in Zanetti's first XI.

Although Oumar Solet has overcome a calf problem, the visitors will be without Alessandro Zanoli, David Alaba and Idrissa Gueye; the matchwinner when these teams recently clashed in the cup, Vakoun Bayo remains a doubt.

Stankovic; Halhal, Moreno, Jesus; Hainaut, Perez, Busio, Sohm, Mazzocchi; Yeboah, Adams

Okoye; Abankwah, Solet, Palma; Vojvoda, Karlstrom, Piotrowski, Kamara; Zaniolo, Ekkelenkamp; Davis

With the worst goal difference and fewest points in Serie A, this pair have been in desperate form for most of the season.

Perhaps a 'new manager bounce' can help Udinese finally stir from their malaise, as Zanetti makes a triumphant return to the Penzo.

#2171. Will Lisandro Martinez play against Leeds after explosive rant?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Lisandro Martinez could return to Manchester United’s starting line-up against Leeds United this weekend, with the defender reportedly available for selection after the controversy surrounding his comments about the club’s summer transfer business.

TEAMtalk transfer insider Graeme Bailey has suggested Michael Carrick could bring the Argentine straight back into the team for the trip to Elland Road. The fixture is expected to be a hostile occasion, making Martinez’s potential involvement an interesting one.

“I am told that Martinez is available for selection this weekend, and there is every chance Michael Carrick starts him,” Bailey said.

The defender recently found himself in trouble after questioning whether Manchester United had done enough in the summer transfer window. He apologised to the club’s hierarchy and was fined, but the incident has not apparently created a divide between him and the manager.

Bailey also dismissed suggestions that Martinez’s comments were an attack on Michael Carrick, insisting the defender and his teammates continue to support the head coach.

“Martinez was not critical of Carrick – far from it. This isn’t a repeat of something like Mo Salah criticising Arne Slot last season. It is almost the opposite.”

According to Bailey, the United dressing room remains firmly behind Carrick despite the team’s disappointing start to the campaign. Senior players reportedly believe the current manager is still the right person to lead the team, rather than bringing in another face to replace him.

“Martinez and the squad are 100% behind Carrick. They know there is pressure after a poor start to the season, but they do not want to see a change.”

Bailey went on to suggest that the fallout may have strengthened the bond between the players and their manager.

“Martinez and the senior players firmly believe nobody can come in and do a better job than Carrick. If anything, this has brought the group closer together.”

#2170. Derek McInnes on Findlay Curtis Rangers future as he reveals player stance

Derek McInnes has made it clear Rangers want Findlay Curtis to commit his long-term future to the club, with the youngster also determined to remain at Ibrox and win trophies.

The 20-year-old is out of contract in the summer and faces stiff competition for regular starts in McInnes' side.

However, the Rangers boss has been impressed with Curtis' attitude and contribution and believes the academy graduate is firmly on the path towards establishing himself as a first-team regular.

"He knows how much we want him to be here longer term," said McInnes. "The club have made that clear to him as well.

"And Findlay's telling me that he wants to be here. He wants to win trophies here. This is his club.

"He knows what we think about him. I'm not here to say to him, 'be patient', because I want him to be in a hurry to get more starts.

"But I believe that he is well on his way to being a good Rangers player going forward."

The Celtic youngster Martin O'Neill was keen to check on during Ross County clash Real Madrid welcomes sporting royalty as tennis icon joins board of directors

McInnes insists there is no shame in Curtis having to battle for minutes given the competition for places at Ibrox, but has challenged the youngster to make the most of every opportunity.

"There's no shame in getting into this Rangers team week in, week out," he said. "He's got good competition there.

"For any player, whether it's homegrown or not, they've got to accept that at Rangers there should be competition for places.

"You don't waste your minutes. If you're in the team, do your best to stay in the team.

"Findlay has contributed really well for us, really strongly. He is a good trainer. He's a brilliant kid."

McInnes also revealed he has enjoyed working with Curtis since taking charge and highlighted the importance of the minutes he has already accumulated, whether from the start or off the bench.

"Getting those minutes is vitally important," he added. "Hopefully this season he can get the minutes that he's happy with, but we're certainly happy with a lot of his contributions."

#2169. Cardinale: “Milan were at the top of the world, and we’re going to bring them back there”

AC Milan owner Gerry Cardinale spoke to CBS Sports before the derby win over Inter, pledging to bring the club back to the summit of football.

On Halloween night, the Rossoneri ensured that Inter’s derby nightmare continued as Milan won their fourth consecutive victory in the fixture, extending the unbeaten run against the Nerazzurri to eight meetings in the process.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

All of the damage was done in the first half. Christian Pulisic struck in the fourth minute with a header, Adrien Rabiot scored from a corner six minutes later, Alessandro Bastoni halved the deficit and then Diego Moreira converted a lovely free-kick to reopen the two-goal advantage.

In the end, 3-1 is how it would stay, securing a memorable win. Cardinale spoke with the CBS panel in the build-up to kick-off at San Siro last night and he shed a bit more light on his plans for the Rossoneri moving forward. His words are via MilanNews.

“Well, first of all, this stadium… As an American, I can say that a stadium like this is something you never see anywhere else. Tonight, it’s going to light up. There’s something different about this derby tonight than in the past.

“There’s an electricity here that I’ve never felt before. You’ll see nearly 80,000 people literally shaking this structure. It’s going to be truly incredible.”

“It is. You know, when we bought the club four years ago, I started working on this right away. And part of what we’re trying to do here is bring the best of America to Italian soccer, and specifically to AC Milan.

“It’s extraordinary: most people didn’t think we could accomplish all this, or even get to this point. Not only did we announce the stadium this week, but we also announced the largest naming rights deal in history. I think it’s a wonderful thing for Italian soccer.

“The message to everyone is that we’re coming back. Italian soccer truly deserves to be at the top again, and we’ll do everything we can through AC Milan to make that happen.”

“There’s a connection between us. We’re two guys from Pennsylvania trying to make their way and understand Italy. He’s ‘Captain America’, but he’s also my franchise player. I couldn’t be more thrilled to have him with us.

“It’s a true partnership with him. He’s a leader, and his presence on the court makes all the difference in the world. You’ve seen it this year: when he steps on the court, he completely changes the game.”

Why is AC Milan and winning with Milan so important to you?

“I remember George Steinbrenner telling me about twenty years ago, ‘Don’t get too emotionally involved in all this; don’t go into the locker room, don’t do those things’. But in this case, I got emotionally involved.

“For me, it became a personal issue. And I don’t know what it is: maybe it’s just that… look around, listen. This means so much to the people of Milan, it has a huge impact on them, and I feel a huge responsibility.

“AC Milan were at the top of the world, and we’re going to bring them back there. This will probably be one of the greatest things I’ll do in my career.”

“It’s something multigenerational. This stadium is an icon. Look around, look at these people: this means everything to them. All of this carries with it an enormous amount of responsibility, it almost makes your wrists tremble.

“The best thing I can do is do my job well to help this club win, because victory spreads to the whole community. Listen… can you hear? That’s the Curva. You don’t see anything like it in America, it’s truly special.”

What’s your favourite detail of the new stadium?

“What we tried to do with the new stadium was to live up to the name and tradition of this facility. This is San Siro, but it is also ‘La Scala del Calcio’, the opera house of football.

“When you look at the design of the new stadium, those majestic, slender lines on the exterior recall the grand drapes and curtains of La Scala. It is our tribute to the history of this city.”

How did you manage to unblock the stadium situation?

“It’s been an incredible collaboration, a true partnership with our president Paolo Scaroni and the mayor. It’s extraordinary because everyone told me it’s impossible to build a stadium here in Italy. The last major stadium built in Italy was in 2011 in Turin, with about 40,000 seats.

“The extraordinary thing about our project is that it will be a 75,000-seat stadium, and we expect to have it completed before the 2032 European Championships. It will be a great thing for all of Italy.”

#2168. Leeds vs Man Utd preview: Team news, form, injuries and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Leeds United welcome Manchester United to Elland Road on Sunday, with both sides looking to put a difficult run of results behind them and get their Premier League season on track.

Leeds United suffered a 2-1 defeat away to Arsenal last weekend despite James Justin scoring to put them in front in the 55th minute.

Manchester United also dropped points in the Premier League, drawing 1-1 with Tottenham, before losing 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in the Champions League away from home.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to find consistency in recent weeks and have come under immense pressure to get in results. The Whites meanwhile will hope that home advantage can make the difference when United face their bitter rivals on Elland Road.

Leeds have recorded one win, two draws and two defeats in their last five matches in all competitions. Their best result came against Newcastle, where they won, but a 6-3 defeat to Chelsea in the Carabao Cup and the loss to Arsenal have left them searching for a response.

Manchester United have also won just once in their last five matches. Their 4-0 Champions League victory over Sabah FK was the only comfortable win, but defeats to Manchester City and Atletico Madrid, alongside draws against Fulham and Tottenham, have raised questions about their form and Michael Carrick.

Leeds are waiting on Dan James, who returned from international duty with a back problem and missed the Arsenal game. Joe Rodon remains sidelined with a hamstring injury and is not expected back in full training until next month. Harry Wilson could return after being an unused substitute last weekend. Mateo Joseph is also among Leeds’ absentees.

Manchester United have their own concerns. Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are doubts after picking up issues against Tottenham and missing Tuesday’s trip to Madrid. Benjamin Sesko remains unavailable with a recurring shin problem, while Patrick Dorgu is expected to be out until late November. Manuel Ugarte is recovering from knee surgery, and Noussair Mazraoui is a doubt with a back problem.

There is better news regarding Matthijs de Ligt, who could return to contention. Leny Yoro is also available for selection despite his Champions League suspension.

The match takes place at Elland Road on Sunday, October 18. Kick-off is scheduled for 2 pm BST (1pm GMT).

UK viewers can watch the game live on Sky Sports Main Event, with coverage beginning at 1 pm BST.

#2167. Sevilla vs Alaves - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 15:53, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 15:58

Sevilla will be aiming to return to winning ways in Spain's top flight when they welcome Alaves to Estadio Ramon on Friday evening.

The home team are currently sixth in the La Liga table, picking up 17 points from 11 matches, while Alaves are 11th in the division, claiming 12 points from 11 games.

Sevilla boast a record of five wins, two draws and four defeats from their 11 league matches this season, with 17 points leaving them in a European spot in the division, sitting sixth.

It has been a difficult period of the season for Luis Garcia Plaza's side, who have only been victorious in one of their last five matches in Spain's top flight.

Sevilla will enter this game off the back of a 3-1 defeat to Getafe, with their last success coming in their last home league fixture - a 2-0 victory over Osasuna.

Los Nervionenses finished 13th in Spain's top flight last season, but they have the look of a team capable of challenging for a European finish this term.

Sevilla have won 21 of their previous 45 matches against Alaves, suffering 12 defeats in the process, but it was 1-1 when the pair last locked horns in February 2026.

Alaves have only actually lost one of their last seven matches against Sevilla, recording four wins in the process, so the visitors should enter this game in a confident mood.

The Blue and Whites suffered a 6-1 defeat to Barcelona last time out, and a tough run of form has seen them pick up just two points from their last seven matches.

Alaves have not been victorious in the league since the start of September, when they recorded a 5-2 win over Osasuna, and their recent struggles have seen them drop down the table, currently sitting 11th with 12 points from 11 matches.

Quique Sanchez Flores' side are just two points off the relegation zone but also only three points behind seventh-placed Athletic Bilbao, so a lot could change with a victory in this match.

Alaves are enjoying a strong period in their history given that nine of their last 10 seasons have been played in the top flight of Spanish football.

Sevilla will be without the services of Manu Bueno, with the midfielder sent off in the team's 3-1 defeat to Getafe last time out.

Bueno had actually been substituted into the match for Isaac Romero, who suffered an injury, and the striker is regarded as a major doubt for this contest.

There will also be absences for Marcao and Miguel Sierra, with the pair unavailable due to muscular and shoulder injuries respectively.

As for Alaves, Facundo Garces and Mikel Rodriguez are both out of the match through injury, but the visitors did not suffer any fresh issues against Barcelona.

Head coach Sanchez Flores is expected to resist the temptation to shuffle his pack despite the disappointing nature of the result against the La Liga champions.

Toni Martinez is expected to be introduced back into the side, while there will be a spot in the final third of the field for Lucas Boye, who has netted five league goals this term.

Vlachodimos; Iglesias, Castrin, Salas, Suazo; Ejuke, Agoume, Fofana, Correia; Stassin, Ure

Sivera; Otto, Tenaglia, Koski, Yusi; Perez, Protesoni, Guevara, Rebbach; Boye, T Martinez

Home advantage could be key in this match, and we are expecting Sevilla to navigate their way to a narrow win in Thursday's league fixture.

#2166. Chelsea duo Estêvão and Hato make Golden Boy shortlist as award rule excludes Lamine Yamal

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Estêvão and Jorrel Hato have reached the final shortlist for the 2026 Golden Boy award, giving Chelsea two contenders.

The organisers’ official announcement confirms their inclusion among Europe’s leading young players.

Arsenal’s Myles Lewis-Skelly, Barcelona defender Pau Cubarsí and Bayern Munich’s Lennart Karl also feature.

Barcelona winger Lamine Yamal is absent because the rules prevent previous winners from claiming the award again.

An international panel of 50 journalists will select the winner, with the announcement scheduled for November.

Estêvão’s nomination follows a productive finish to his international window. He scored and supplied an assist in Brazil’s 4-0 victory over India on Saturday.

The winger now returns to Chelsea with those contributions behind him as he seeks more opportunities under Xabi Alonso.

Hato provides a different profile among Chelsea’s nominees. The Dutch defender can operate at centre-back or on the left, with his versatility offering Alonso options alongside Pep Chavarria.

Both remain in contention for the individual honour, although nomination alone does not establish either as the favourite.

#2164. Willian admits Chelsea exit was a ‘big mistake’ after wanting Arsenal escape just THREE months later

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Willian has described leaving Chelsea for Arsenal as a mistake, explaining how quickly his enthusiasm for the move turned into regret.

Speaking to former team-mate John Obi Mikel on The Obi One Podcast, the Brazilian revisited the decision that ended his seven-year Stamford Bridge spell in 2020.

Chelsea offered two more years, while Willian wanted three. His contract-length disagreement with Chelsea featured in ReadChelsea’s coverage at the time.

Arsenal offered the longer deal. Willian also valued keeping his family in London and says Mikel Arteta outlined a central role for him.

“To be honest I should have stayed,” he said, in comments reported by the Mirror.

The winger followed the same route as Petr Cech, who explained his own Chelsea-to-Arsenal move through the desire for regular football.

Willian says the reality differed from the picture presented during talks. Within three months, he had told his agent he wanted to leave.

He linked that frustration to his performances, explaining that unhappiness prevented him from producing his best football.

His Arsenal spell ultimately lasted one season. He scored once in 37 appearances before leaving for Corinthians in 2021.

#2163. Jamie Carragher names Man Utd Star who impressed him against Leeds

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United had a difficult evening once again as they were held to a 1-1 draw at Leeds United. Pressure mounts on Michael Carrick as United have just 7 points from 7 games.

Manchester United took the lead through Bryan Mbeumo in the first half, with the forward finishing Youri Tielemans’ excellent cross from the right. However, the visitors couldn’t hold on to their advantage as Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised for Leeds United from a corner.

The draw extended United’s frustrating run of results, leaving Michael Carrick still searching for the consistency his side need.

Carragher, though, believes there were positives to take from the trip, particularly one performance at the back.

Jamie Carragher has singled out Harry Maguire for praise after Manchester United drew 1-1 with Leeds United at Elland Road on Sunday.

Speaking on Sky Sports, the former Liverpool defender admitted that picking up a point at Elland Road was not necessarily a bad result in isolation, even if United’s recent form made it feel like a missed opportunity.

“Going away to Leeds is going to be tricky for any team except for Manchester City and Arsenal at the moment,” Carragher said.

“A lot of teams will take a point at Leeds this season but the problem for Manchester United is the results have been so poor that they need to start winning games so it almost feels like two points dropped.”

Carragher then turned his attention to Maguire, who he felt played a major role in keeping United in the game when Leeds began to push for a winner.

“But in isolation, getting a point at Leeds is a decent result. I thought Harry Maguire was fantastic, especially in the second half.”

Leeds United grew into the contest after Calvert-Lewin brought them level, putting United under pressure during the closing stages. Carragher felt Maguire’s efforts in defence helped prevent the visitors from falling apart.

“After Leeds got that goal back there was a stage of the game where it felt like Man United could go under, but Maguire at the back held it together for them. He was fantastic.”

The result may feel like another opportunity missed for Carrick, but Maguire’s performance gave United something positive to take away from Elland Road.

#2162. 'Maybe I will cry': Oh reflects on 'hard time' at Celtic before emotional return

Oh Hyeon-gyu departed Celtic for Genk in 2024 after falling out of favour under Brendan Rodgers.

Following 18 goal-laden months in Belgium, the South Korean striker joined Besiktas for €14 million at the start of this year.

In 34 appearances for the Turkish side, Oh has scored 12 goals.

It's a tally he wants to increase against his former employers at Celtic Park in the Europa League on Thursday evening.

"First of all, it's very special for me to be back here," Oh said. "I couldn't speak English properly back then. But everyone welcomed me like family here. And Celtic became like my second home.

"Maybe I will be emotional tomorrow. I'd be happy to score tomorrow. I'm Besiktas now. I will give everything for Besiktas."

Oh joined Celtic in January 2023 from Suwon Bluewings in his homeland.

"What can I say? I came here as a young boy with a dream," he continued. "It was sometimes hard for me, sometimes good for me.

"I have got good memories here. I will always be grateful for everything and the fans.

Now 25, Oh has progressed both on and off the pitch since swapping South Korea for Scotland almost four years ago.

"I learned a lot about my position from Kyogo Furuhashi and Adam Idah," Oh added. "It was a hard time for me because I didn't play a lot, sometimes I played, sometimes I did not.

"In my last season here, I didn't play for the last six months so it was a hard time for me. But I'm very happy to be back here because I love Celtic so much. It has a special place in my heart."

Ahead of Thursday's match, Besiktas boss Vincenzo Italiano said: "We have got points already but this is another important game.

"We are a team that’s very hungry. There may be changes to our team but we will give everything to get a positive result.

"We have been going well in our European games, winning our last game 3-0.  We have a different attention span for these games and have done a good job. It has not been easy but we have worked really hard. We are focused on qualifying on this phase and this game will be very tough.

"It’s a an amazing stadium and we are going to play against a very strong team with nice supporters. Celtic will want to win because they haven’t collected points yet.

"We have to be as hungry as them because it’s going to be a very difficult game. I played against Celtic at Bologna and it was a fantastic night. But this will be different.

"I am very much surprised they have zero points so we may see an angry Celtic team. This may complicate our job. It will be an important test for us. Everybody has to be really prepared."

#2161. Oxford City vs Leicester City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 04 Nov 2026 16:25, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 22:11

Fallen giants Leicester City will be aiming to avoid what would be one of the biggest shocks in recent FA Cup history when they head to National League North outfit Oxford City on Friday night in the first round of the tournament.

It is the first time that Leicester have competed in this round since 2008, and Oxford City have been given the chance to claim a huge scalp in the tournament.

Oxford City have managed to progress to the second round of the FA Cup on three previous occasions - 1969-70, 2017-18 and 2020-21, so the club have previous when it comes to advancing past this stage of the famous cup competition.

The Hoops will enter this match off the back of a disappointing result, though, going down 3-1 at home to South Shields in the National League North on Saturday.

Ross Jenkins' side are currently second in the National League North, picking up 27 points from their opening 14 matches of the campaign, two points behind South Shields following the clash between the top two.

Oxford City have had to win three matches to reach this stage of the FA Cup, overcoming Dorchester, Enfield Town and Torquay to be present in the first round.

Leicester, meanwhile, will enter this match off the back of a 2-2 draw with Stevenage, with the result leaving them in 10th spot in the League One table.

The Foxes have won three of their last five league games, but there is no getting away from the fact that it has been a disappointing start to the season as a whole.

Leicester famously won this competition back in 2021, recording a 1-0 victory over Chelsea courtesy of a second-half effort from Youri Tielemans.

Russell Martin is still under pressure at Leicester but remains at the helm, with the 40-year-old bidding to help the Foxes secure a return to the Championship.

Leicester's last FA Cup fixture brought a 2-1 defeat to Southampton in the last-16 stage of the 2025-26 competition.

Oxford City will be looking to Tyrone Marsh for goals on Friday, with the 32-year-old netting nine times in all competitions at the start of the 2026-27 campaign.

There will also be a position in the team for Scott Pollock, who has been another key performer this season, coming up with six goals and two assists in 13 appearances.

Ludwig Francillette and Josh Ashby are also set to be notable starters.

As for Leicester, there are set to be a number of changes to the team that took to the field for the 2-2 draw with Stevenage in the league last time out.

However, Ben Nelson and Jakub Stolarczyk are both out of the match through injury.

Conor Chaplin should be introduced in the final third of the field, while 19-year-old Ilari Kangasniemi is expected to be a notable starter at the back.

Fellow teenager Louis Page is expected to be among those to be rested.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

After a tentative start in which both teams had a few half-chances, it was the home team took the lead in the 20th minute with a shot from outside the box by Cuel that deceived Pittarella.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

Their last six matches across all competitions have produced three draws and three defeats. Michael Carrick is in pressure of his own and needs a response from his players, particularly with United sitting 13th in the Premier League and 13th in the Champions League standings.

Como have shown more promise. Cesc Fabregas’ side beat RB Leipzig 4-1 in Europe before losing 3-2 to Feyenoord. They returned to winning ways against Fiorentina, claiming a 3-1 victory that moved them up to fifth in Serie A, with 14 points from seven games.

Como have scored 11 goals and conceded eight across their last six matches in all competitions. United have struggled, with their only clean sheet of the season coming against Sabah FK.

United have several confirmed absentees ahead of the trip to Italy. Patrick Dorgu, Manuel Ugarte and Benjamin Sesko are ruled out, while Leny Yoro is suspended for the European fixture after picking a red against Atletico Madrid.

Lisandro Martinez is a major concern after coming off at half-time against Leeds with a thigh problem. Ayden Heaven, Luke Shaw and Kobbie Mainoo are all doubtful for the game.

Como will be without Assane Diao, who is sidelined with a thigh injury. Moise Kean has a shin problem and is a doubt, while Trevoh Chalobah is also a fitness concern with a muscular issue.

The match takes place at the Stadio Giuseppe Sinigaglia on Wednesday, October 21, with kick-off at 5:45 pm BST.

#2157. Gaziantep vs Kasimpasa - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joshua Cole | 04 Nov 2026 16:42, Last updated: 04 Nov 2026 16:42

Gaziantep will be desperate to halt their alarming slide when they welcome Kasimpasa to Gaziantep Stadyumu on Friday in the Turkish Super Lig.

The hosts have lost five consecutive league matches and are hovering just above the relegation zone with eight points from their opening 10 fixtures, while their visitors occupy fifth place with 17 points and arrive unbeaten in their last three league outings.

Gaziantep have endured a miserable spell in the league, with their latest setback coming in a 2-0 defeat away to Trabzonspor.

Orhan Ak's side struggled to make an impact against the Black Sea outfit, conceding once in each half as they suffered another damaging result following defeats against Erzurumspor, Istanbul Basaksehir, Corum and Kocaelispor.

Their 4-0 Turkish Cup victory over Etimesgutspor provided some relief, but that result has done little to disguise their domestic difficulties, particularly as they have scored just twice across their last five league matches while conceding 12 goals.

The lack of attacking output is becoming a significant concern for Ak, whose team have struggled to turn possession into meaningful opportunities and have repeatedly found themselves chasing matches after falling behind.

Gaziantep have also struggled to establish Gaziantep Stadyumu as a source of points, failing to win any game as hosts yet, drawing two and losing three times in five home matches.

However, the Falcons are unbeaten in their last five meetings with the Apaches, picking up three wins and two draws, while they have also avoided defeat in their last three encounters against them as hosts, winning twice and drawing once.

Kasimpasa, meanwhile, have enjoyed a considerably more encouraging opening to the campaign, losing just one of their 10 league games so far while recording four wins and five draws.

Emre Belozoglu's team were held to an entertaining 2-2 draw by Besiktas in their previous league outing, with Claudio Winck and Michael Rafferty finding the net as they twice threatened to take all three points.

That result extended their unbeaten league sequence to three matches, following victories over Eyupspor and Samsunspor, while they also recorded a 3-1 win over Corluspor in the Turkish Cup, extending their unbeaten run across all competitions to four matches.

Kasimpasa have also collected useful points on their travels this season, recording 10 points from five away games, with three wins, one draw and one defeat, which should give them optimism ahead of this trip.

Gaziantep have several availability concerns ahead of Friday's encounter, with Myenty Abena facing suspension following a yellow-card accumulation, while Ulrich Meleke, Ogun Ozcicek, Arda Kizildag and Sontje Hansen are among those carrying injuries.

Serdar Dursun is also expected to miss out, while Nazim Sangare and Fuat Bavuk remain unavailable.

Kasimpasa have fewer confirmed concerns, although Kamil Ahmet Corekci is expected to remain sidelined with an Achilles tendon injury.

Godfried Frimpong and Kevin Mouanga are also carrying fitness problems, leaving the visitors potentially short of defensive options, while no further suspensions have been reported.

Adrian Benedyczak has scored six goals in the league this season, and he will be the man to watch for the Apaches in this encounter.

Dadakdeniz; Perez, Abena, Sylla, Calhanoglu; Bacuna; Sorescu, Gidado, Maxim, Kozlowski; Stewart

Gianniotis; Jensen, Ilie, Arous, Winck; Demirbay, Mendes; Hajradinovic, Rak-Sakyi, Kol; Benedyczak

Gaziantep's home advantage and historical record in this fixture could make Friday's encounter more competitive than the league standings suggest, but their current defensive problems are difficult to overlook.

Kasimpasa have shown considerably more attacking consistency, and their recent form should ultimately see them claim all three points.

#2155. Rio Ferdinand disappointed Man Utd didn’t sign midfielder better than Elliot Anderson

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Rio Ferdinand has admitted he is disappointed that Manchester United failed to sign Ayyoub Bouaddi, with the highly-rated midfielder now making an impression at Manchester City.

Manchester City beat several rivals to secure Bouaddi’s signature over the summer in a deal worth £86 million. The Morocco international became the Premier League’s most expensive teenager, and Rio Ferdinand believes Manchester United missed out on a special talent.

The former United defender was particularly impressed by Bouaddi’s second-half cameo during City’s Champions League defeat to Paris Saint-Germain last week. His performance reminded Rio Ferdinand why he had been talking about the youngster throughout the previous year and during the summer transfer window.

Speaking on his YouTube channel, Ferdinand highlighted how Bouaddi was the highlight of the game against PSG and who they missed.

“He came on and showed PSG that they may have missed out on someone who was on their doorstep when he was playing in France,” Ferdinand said.

The former England international also confirmed that Manchester United had been interested in bringing the midfielder to Old Trafford, but the transfer fee was too expensive.

“Manchester United were in for him, 100%, but the fee was just too much,” he added.

Ferdinand was left frustrated by what he saw from the teenager, who turned 19 this month.

“Just that little cameo against PSG makes you go, ‘oh, I can’t believe we missed out on that. I can’t believe it.’ I’m so gutted, so gutted. He’s bad! He’s so calm under pressure.”

Rio Ferdinand suggested Bouaddi’s quality could soon make the midfield position in a packed Manchester City his regular role.

City already have Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez among their midfield options, but Ferdinand believes Bouaddi has the ability to stand out alongside them.

“They’ve got Elliot Anderson and Enzo Fernandez in that midfield as well but this kid could be the best of the three, easily.”

#2154. Hearts reveal investment in push for European return resulted in £2.4m loss

Hearts have announced a loss of £2.4million after investing heavily to return to European football this season.

The Tynecastle club backed Derek McInnes after he took over as boss in the summer of 2025.

The Jambos’ thrilling William Hill Premiership title challenge went to the last day of the season when they lost to Celtic at Parkhead to finish second to the Hoops.

Hearts lost to Sturm Graz in the Champions League qualifiers and then to Benfica in the Europa League, but victory over Rapid Vienna saw Wouter Vrancken’s side make it to the league phase of the Conference League, where they face Swiss side Thun on Thursday.

Hearts also reported a turnover of £23.1m for the 2025/26 financial year, are also in “positive” discussions about further investment to help Vrancken in the January transfer window and are confident of a “significantly improved financial result” in a year’s time.

Derek McInnes on Findlay Curtis Rangers future as he reveals player stance The Celtic youngster Martin O'Neill was keen to check on during Ross County clash

The club’s financial results also revealed that staff costs went up eight per cent “with player acquisition costs of £7.8m across the year, reflecting significant investment in the playing squad”.

A statement on Hearts’ official website said: “The club took a deliberate decision to invest in the playing squad from a position of financial strength, helping secure a return to European league phase football for 2026/27.

“This additional investment in squad wages, combined with the absence of European income, resulted in an operating loss of £2.4m for the year.

“With European income returning and further progress in player trading, the club anticipates a significantly improved financial result in 2026/27.

“Strong underlying revenue growth offset much of the loss of European income for the 2025/26 financial year, with sponsorship and advertising income up 37 per cent and retail and hospitality income up 17 per cent, reflecting continued commercial progress and support from partners and fans alike.

“The club continued to benefit from exceptional financial backing from its benefactors and Foundation of Hearts members, and extends its sincere thanks for their continued commitment and support.

“The increased investment in the playing squad and football operations was supported by an investment from Tony Bloom towards the end of the previous financial year, which strengthened the club’s financial position and provided capacity to invest in improving the playing squad.

“As an equity investment, this funding is reflected in the balance sheet rather than recognised as income in the profit and loss account.

“The reported operating loss of £2.4m includes £1.8m of gains from player trading and is stated before depreciation, amortisation and finance costs.

“Further gains will be reflected in the 2026/27 financial year following player trading activity completed in July and August 2026.

“The club is also engaged in positive discussions regarding further investment to support the continued, measured pursuit of the club’s ambitions.

“The club’s balance sheet remains robust, with the accounts reporting net assets of £20.3m at the June 30 2026 financial year-end.”

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick has come under immense pressure as a disjointed Manchester United sit just two places above the relegation places.

Despite the scrutiny and the news of a possible sacking, Michael Carrick has been given another chance at Manchester United.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

It has been reported by Sky Sports that United have considered other options internally as well to replace Micahel Carrick but for the weekend, they are sticking with Michael Carrick.

Carrick took training on Tuesday and the decision means he has at least one more match to convince the club’s hierarchy that he can get United back on track.

Gary Neville admitted he was surprised that Carrick had survived the immediate threat of losing his job, although he welcomed the decision.

“I am surprised, but I am happy because I was absolutely certain that what I witnessed on Sunday would be the end of Michael,” Neville said in Sky Sports.

The former United captain explained that Carrick’s actions after the Bournemouth defeat had made him think the manager was preparing to say goodbye to the supporters.

“It looked like he was walking round at the end on his own with the players behind him, like it was almost a goodbye lap,” Neville added.

Fans continued singing the manager’s name after the final whistle, while the rest of the team drew boos.

Despite his surprise, Gary Neville hopes Micahel Carrick can use the extra time to get a response from his squad.

“I hope he can somehow get those players to start playing for him again because they haven’t played for him in the last few weeks,” he said.

The trip to Chelsea now becomes a crucial test for Micahel Carrick but United need a result and a far better performance to ease the pressure at Old Trafford.

Mateta's contract at Palace is due to expire at the end of the campaign, and there are currently no indications that he will be signing a fresh deal.

The Frenchman is seemingly looking for a way out at Palace and is said to be keen to explore a switch to the 20-time English champions next year.

A loan move for the striker is unlikely given his contract situation, but he could potentially be available for a bargain transfer fee in the winter market.

#2149. Chelsea sweat on key stars as Bournemouth test looms

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Chelsea return to Premier League action this weekend with a growing treatment table and a lot of questions for Xabi Alonso to answer before Saturday’s visit of Bournemouth.

The main concern revolves around Joao Pedro. Chelsea’s current player of the season rescued a point against newly promoted Hull, but he suffered a knock and sat out the damaging 3-0 defeat at Brentford. The problem also prompted his removal from Brazil’s squad shortly after he had returned to it.

It is understood that he is undergoing specialist knee treatment in an attempt to expedite his recovery, although reports in Brazil suggest that he could be out for up to four weeks. No date has yet been set, but a return later in October looks most likely.

There is more good news elsewhere. Cole Palmer was among the players called up to the latest England squad, which did not go down too well with Thomas Tuchel.

The midfielder has been recovering from a persistent groin injury and has been working with a rehab coach. He is likely to be fit this weekend.

Moises Caicedo has also struggled with an injury this season, playing just 14 minutes across the campaign. He’s spent the majority of time on the bench recovering from a bad calf injury, which ruled him out for most of the season.

He made his only appearance as a substitute against his former club Brighton, but the game was red-flagged for Ecuador’s friendlies, so he did not play.

Alonso said before the Brentford match that the player would be ready to return. It seems to be the case, unless something went wrong.

Captain Reece James missed the Brentford game with yet another hamstring problem. Alonso said he only just missed out, so a return seems likely.

There may also be debuts. Marco Palestra, the £27m signing from Atalanta, has been sidelined since a training injury just before the opening match against Fulham. He is back in full training and was expected to feature in a behind-closed-doors friendly with Portsmouth. Chelsea lost 2-0. Emmanuel Emegha, injured after scoring against Bromley in July, is also pushing for involvement.

Less clear is the position of Jamie Gittens. He limped off with an ankle injury playing for England Under-21s against Kazakhstan and was seen on crutches. Mike Penders is fine after a quad scare on Belgium duty. Returning to the bench against Turkey.

Chelsea badly need a lift. Defeat at Brentford before the break left Alonso’s side looking shaky, and a squad stretched by injuries has not helped.

Saturday brings a chance to steady things. If Palmer, James and Caicedo are available, with Palestra and Emegha potentially adding fresh options, the picture will look much brighter at Stamford Bridge. Joao Pedro’s absence will still sting. However, a strong response against Bournemouth would go a long way towards easing the pressure and restoring belief.

#2148. Sparta Prague vs. Bournemouth: How to watch Europa League fixture, date, time, live stream and TV channel

By Ben Sully | 04 Nov 2026 17:03, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 03:26

Bournemouth will continue their maiden European campaign in Thursday's meeting with Sparta Prague in the Europa League league phase.

The Cherries are sitting in third place in the Europa League standings, while the hosts start matchday four in 16th position.

Here, Sports Mole provides all the key details about how to tune into the European fixture.

The game will get underway at 18:45pm local time on Thursday, which will be a 17:45 kick-off for UK viewers.

Bournemouth will travel to Stadion Letna, which can hold over 18,000 spectators.

Sparta have won six of their nine competitive home matches this season (D1, L2), although they have failed to win two of their last three games in front of their supporters.

UK viewers with the required subscription can watch the game on TNT Sports 2.

Alternatively, you can catch the game on the HBO Max website and app with a TNT Sports subscription. The HBO Max app is available on a wide range of devices, including phones, tablets, smart TVs and game consoles.

There is also the option to purchase a TNT Sports subscription as an add-on on Amazon Prime Video.

There will be match highlights available on the HBO Max website and app, as well as the TNT Sports Football YouTube channel.

The @footballontnt X account will also share key moments from Thursday's contest.

After featuring in the Conference League last 16 last term, Sparta are aiming to make it back-to-back European knockout appearances in this season's Europa League.

Brian Priske's side are currently sitting in a seeded spot for the knockout round playoffs, with three points separating them from the top eight and a one-point gap to the teams sitting just outside the top 24.

In regard to their overall form, Sparta will be looking to spark an upturn in fortunes after winning just one of their last six competitive matches.

In contrast to their hosts, Bournemouth carry significant momentum into the midweek fixture after winning each of their last six competitive matches without conceding, including a 2-0 Premier League win over Leeds United on Saturday.

Marco Rose's side are looking to maintain their 100% Europa League record after starting their European campaign with three successive wins over Real Sociedad, Sturm Graz and AC Milan.

Another win on Thursday would further strengthen their hopes of a top-eight finish and an automatic last-16 spot.
